# The Music of the Primes: Lean map

For each result the manuscript's argument relies on, and each issue in `ISSUES.md`, the checked Lean declarations involved and what each proves. Every declaration named here is listed in `MANIFEST.tsv` with its source file under `lean/` and its compiler receipt. Page numbers refer to the PDF of the manuscript.

Columns: *Proves* states the conclusion; *Objects* names the Lean constructions the statement is about; *Scope* gives the range (finite rank or full operator, fields, characters, tests); *Hypotheses* lists the assumptions of the statement.

<a id="result-1"></a>
## Result 1 — §3.2.1–3.2.2: (3.2.1.7), Theorem 3.2.2.1

For every number field K, every unitary Hecke character χ (continuous Circle-valued character of the ideles trivial on principal ideles; no restriction on norm twists) and every s with Re s > 1: the function a ↦ f_χ(a_f)·χ(a)·∥a∥_f^s on the finite-idele subgroup {a: a_∞ = 1} is integrable for the Haar measure normalized by mass 1 on the integral units, its integral equals the sum of the integrals over the valuation shells, and it equals finiteHeckeL K χ s = ∏'_v heckeEulerFactor (1 − χ_v(ϖ_v)N(v)^{−s})^{−1} at unramified v and 1 at ramified v. The test f_χ is the restricted tensor of the local tests 1_{O_v} at unramified v and χ_v^{−1}·1_{O_v^×} at ramified v. The Euler product converges to a nonzero holomorphic function on Re s > 1, with the logarithmic-derivative and prime-power identities there; it is independent of the choice of uniformizers; the dual character gives the inverse-coefficient product.

## Result 2 — Proposition 3.2.1.3, Theorem 3.2.2.1, Theorem 3.4.1.1

For general K the checked statements are conditional bridges for a supplied continuation: an entire (or meromorphic) continuation agreeing with the Euler germ on Re s > 1 is unique (as a function, resp. as punctured germs), and two such pole-cleared candidates with the two Gamma normalizations differ by the explicit constant ∏_v (1 if real, 2 if complex); the full divisor of ∏ Γ_ℝ(z+r_i)∏ Γ_ℂ(z+c_j) is computed (−1 at each pole of each factor, coincident poles added); a supplied meromorphic L whose Gamma-completed product is a WeakFEPair Mellin transform on Re z > 1 has the completed order = Gamma ledger + ord L; for a WeakFEPair with k = 1 and nonzero constants the shifted Λ(z+iτ) is meromorphic on ℂ with simple poles at −iτ, 1−iτ; a completed FE of the form L(z) = e^{a−bz}L̃(1−z) gives L′/L(s) = −b − L̃′/L̃(1−s); every unitary Hecke character corresponds bijectively to a continuous character of the idele class group (a locally declared `GroundTheory.Global.HeckeCharacter` type). For K = ℚ the checked statements use Mathlib's continued Dirichlet and Riemann zeta functions with norm twists: analyticity off s+iτ = 1, L = Λ/Γ-factor for Re s > 0 with the Gamma factor nonzero there, entire pole-cleared functions, and nonvanishing for Re s > 1.

## Result 3 — Zero divisor (Chapters 3, 9, 13)

For K = ℚ (Mathlib's Riemann zeta and Dirichlet L-functions with norm twists): analytic orders are finite at every point of the regular domain, the multiplicities of L and of its completion agree in the open strip, the divisor on any compact subset of the regular domain is the analytic order, zeros in compact sets are finite and zeros are countable; zero counts in bands and below height T are bounded by Jensen circle means (Issue 9). For general supplied functions: finite orders from a nonzero half-plane model at 2; Gamma-factor and norm-twist polar divisors (Result 2, Issue 24); summability Σ μ_i/h_i² from a weighted T(1+log T) count; the logarithmic derivative of the genus-one Weierstrass factor (1 − z/ρ)e^{z/ρ}; summability of omitted-copy and missing-jet series given Σ ∣ρ_j∣^{−2} < ∞; an entire function not identically zero has finitely many zeros in each closed ball.

<a id="result-4"></a>
## Result 4 — Theorem 10.2.2.1

On the actual idele class group C_K of every number field, for every unitary Hecke character χ and every measure mu whose classLog-pushforward is a nonzero finite multiple of Lebesgue measure (this holds for every Haar measure, `RIntegrated.originalHaar_classLog_map`): if ρ satisfies the strict admission ∣Re ρ − a∣ < α, or = α with 2m+1 < s, and a supplied function L is analytic at ρ with finite order > m, and for every test f in a dense submodule D of a parametric test space V (with S₀ = ker d ∩ ker q and pivots u, v ∈ D) the zeroth native Mellin integral ∫ χ∣x∣^{w−a}Φ_f dmu equals L(w)·Q_f(w) near ρ with Q_f analytic, then the conjugated normalized jet vector (the Riesz representative of η_{χ,ρ−a,m} in the weighted space) lies in the orthogonal complement of the closed linear span of the theta image {theta f: f ∈ S₀}. In the most general version the derivative interchange ∫ jet_m·Φ_f = (d/dw)^m ∫ jet_0·Φ_f is proved in the open strip and is a hypothesis at the strip edge, and continuity is a hypothesis for the scalar pairing f ↦ ∫ jet·Φ_f. For an indexed family of such jets, if the reverse inclusion (closed span of theta)ᗮ ⊆ closed span of the jets is supplied as a hypothesis (residue exhaustion), the two closed subspaces are equal. Supporting facts on the same objects: weighted-L² membership of admitted jets (interior and boundary, Issue 11), linear independence of jet families across characters, exponents and orders (Issue 12), and the derivative identity for the actual class theta function of a test with f(0) = f̂(0) = 0 at every z (given Poisson, continuity and one-sided decay as hypotheses).

## Result 5 — Theorems 10.3.1.1, 10.3.2.1, 10.4.2.1

Source-path prefixes (relative to SP): NR =; AZ =; EXP =.
Notation used in the table: β = max(|u|,|v|) + s/4; #F = number of elements of the finite type F; ℓ = classLog (log of the idele modulus on C_K); L = Σ_{n∈ℤ} (1+|n|)^{−2} (`Round196H.latticeMass`); w_d(k) = ∏_{j<d} (1+|k_j|)^{−2} (`latticeWeight`); NativeDualFiber u v s = L²(ℝ, e^{−2max(ux,vx)−(s/2)log(1+x²)} dx) (`Round195D.NativeDualFiber`, docstring: "The actual weighted dual log-coordinate Hilbert fiber, not yet the full character sum"); U(t) = `Round196D.nativeTranslation u v s hs t`, the translation f ↦ f(·+t) on that fiber; nuclearNorm T = infimum of Σ‖f_i‖‖v_i‖ over representations T x = Σ f_i(x) v_i with Σ‖f_i‖‖v_i‖ < ∞ (`Round195H.nuclearNorm`, defined in the source; docstring: "This is a definition, not an assumed trace-class interface").

- Finite-level nuclear bound. `Round197H.native_finite_level_resolvent_nuclear_bound` proves nuclearNorm T ≤ (D/(Re z − β))·#F·L^d for an operator T on an arbitrary Hilbert space H which is, by hypothesis, the strongly convergent sum over a finite-level character index set F × ℤ^d of blocks inc_χ R_χ R_χ W_χ pr_χ, each block acting on a closed subspace V_χ of the constructed fiber NativeDualFiber u v s, where R_χ satisfies the resolvent identity for the constructed translation generator at z (Re z > β, s ≥ 0), the finite real-trace sums of R_χ on a Hilbert basis of V_χ are ≤ Z_χ, ‖W_χ‖ ≤ B_χ and B_χ Z_χ ≤ D·w_d(k). Inside the proof the Hilbert–Schmidt column estimate Σ‖R_χ b_i‖² ≤ Z_χ/(Re z − β) is derived from the growth bound ‖U(t)‖ ≤ e^{β|t|} of the constructed translation group (`Round196E`, `Round195E`), the row estimate is derived from the column estimate, the per-block cost B_χ·Z_χ/(Re z − β) comes from Young's inequality for R·(R W), and the summation over F × ℤ^d is derived from the proved lattice majorant (`Round196H`). Docstring: "The column and row estimates are derived, not assumed. Native finite traces, character decay and the original operator identity are still explicit residuals; this is not the full number-field theorem."
- Transfer from the quotient. For any bounded A preserving a subspace G (with orthogonal projection) of a complex inner product space, the nuclear representation cost of P_{G^⊥}∘A is at most that of the induced quotient operator Ā on H/G, and every rank-one expansion of Ā transports through Q and the orthogonal section S to an expansion of P_{G^⊥}∘A of no larger cost (`Round175H.canonical_projected_nuclear_cost_le`, `Round174A.projected_quotient_series_transfer`; this is the reduction (10.4.2.5) with the costs made explicit).
- Finite rank, both tests. Each finite projected jet block S∘(Σ_{m<n} jetAction(η,c,m)⊗ψ_m)∘Q is an explicit finite rank-one sum with cost ≤ Σ_{m<n}Σ_{i≤m} C(m,i)‖c_i‖‖η_{m−i}‖‖ψ_m‖ (`Round175A`). For both tests φ# and (ℓφ)# on the actual idele class group HeckeIdeleClasses K of any number field, with c_i the actual Mellin moments ∫ h·χ·e^{zℓ}·ℓ^i dμ, one support radius R gives this cost with ‖c_i‖ replaced by e^{αR}R^i∫|φ#|dμ (resp. e^{αR}R^{i+1}∫|φ#|dμ), uniformly in the unimodular continuous χ, in |Re z| ≤ α, in n and in the vectors η, ψ (`Round177G`, `Round177H`); for a log-coordinate test f ∈ C_c^∞(ℝ) and both log shifts r = 0, 1, ‖z‖^N times the block cost is bounded with factors e^{αR}R^{i+r}(i+r+1)^N∫|f^{(N−k)}| (`Round180D`, `Round180G`, `Round179G`). The costs keep the factors ‖η_{m−i}‖·‖ψ_m‖ and are stated per block, not summed over blocks.
- Finite windows of the projected operator. When vectors r_i have common orthogonal complement G and A(G) ⊆ G, P_{G^⊥}∘A∘P_{window(r,J)} has finite rank-one cost ≤ dim(window(r,J))·‖P_{G^⊥}∘A‖ for every finite J, and P_{G^⊥}A(P_{window(r,J)}x) → P_{G^⊥}A x for every x (`Round177E.finite_spectral_input_approximation`); the finite triangular matrices converge strongly to P_{G^⊥}∘A (`Round177F`, docstring: "This is strong convergence; it is explicitly not S1 convergence.").
- On the actual objects of the (L²(C_K, μ) realization of W_I^s, I = [l, r], with the native half weight; `intervalConvolutionCLM` for both tests; P = 1 − projection onto `fullThetaClosure`): a nuclear expansion of A∘P yields a nuclear expansion of P∘A with no larger cost, together with the physical-cutoff trace limit (`Q.FullSourceCutoff.both_tests_PA_physical_limit_of_AP`); for any M such that A∘M has a nuclear factorization, P∘A∘M is the nuclear-norm limit of the finite-source truncations (1 − E_T)∘A∘M (`Q.FullSourceCutoff.both_tests_full_source_nuclearNorm_tendsto`); P∘A itself is realized with its compression identity, its strong Hilbert-basis expansion, strong cutoff limits and finite-source traces (`PIntegrated.geometric_trace_global_operator_assembly`, docstring: "Nuclear cost and the global local-trace passage are distinct applications, not hypotheses hidden in this statement.").
- Pieces of the convolution operator. Explicit nuclear representations with cost bounds by the L² norms of the kernel factors, and cutoff stability, for residual, commutator, compact-output, strip-crossing and strip-correction pieces of the weight-transported convolution operator on L²(C_K, μ) for tests ψ∗θ (`Round118G.genuine_seven_residual_nuclear_cost` and the declarations listed with it). The projection P_a^spec does not occur in these operators.
- The trace assemblies take a nuclear expansion of P∘A (or of A∘P, or of the compression on G^⊥) as a hypothesis and derive diagonal/trace identities from it; `ConditionalB26.ordered_cutoff_nuclear_trace` also proves the two-sided ideal step C∘(P∘A)∘D nuclear from such an expansion.

<a id="result-6"></a>
## Result 6 — (10.4.2.29)–(10.4.2.33), (10.3.2.3)

Notation used below. C_K = `HeckeIdeleClasses K` = (𝔸_K)^× modulo the principal ideles, with the quotient topology and its Borel σ-algebra. ℓ(g) = `classLog K g`, the logarithm of the normalized idelic modulus ∏_w ‖g_w‖^{mult w}·∏_v ‖g_v‖. χ is a `UnitaryHeckeCharacter K` (continuous homomorphism 𝔸_K^× → circle, trivial on K^×, descended to C_K by `classCharacter`). η^z_m(x) = χ(x)·exp((z−a)ℓ(x))·ℓ(x)^m (`genuineMellinJet K χ z a m`), so η^{a+b+it}_m is the manuscript's η_{χ,t,b,m}. w(x) = exp(2α·abs ℓ(x))·(1+ℓ(x)²)^{s/2} (`Round175B.nativeWeight`), the weight of W^s_I; η̃ = η/w^{1/2} (`genuineNormalizedMellinJet`). A(h)f(x) = ∫ h(g) f(g⁻¹x) dµ(g). C(m,k) is the binomial coefficient. "(z,m) admitted" means Re z − a ∈ (−α, α), or Re z − a = ±α and 2m+1 < s. φ# = `sharp`/`nativeSharp` φ: g ↦ conj φ(g⁻¹); ℓφ = `logTest`/`nativeLogKernel` φ: g ↦ ℓ(g)φ(g).

*Measured jet action.* For every number field K, every unitary Hecke character χ, every z ∈ ℂ and every jet order m, the pointwise binomial law (10.4.2.29)–(10.4.2.33) η^z_m(gy) = Σ_{k≤m} C(m,k) η^z_k(g) η^z_{m−k}(y) holds on the actual idele class group, and the integrated form (10.3.1.8)–(10.3.1.12) ∫ h(g) η^z_m(gy) dµ(g) = Σ_{k≤m} C(m,k) (∫ h η^z_k dµ)·η^z_{m−k}(y) holds for any measure µ and any test h with the k ≤ m moments integrable (integrability derived for continuous compactly supported h and locally finite µ). Paired with the actual left convolution A(h), for µ left-invariant, locally finite and s-finite, h continuous with compact support and f in the weighted space (w^{1/2} f ∈ L²(µ)), the transpose identity ∫ (A(h)f) η^z_m dµ = Σ_{k≤m} C(m,k) (∫ h η^z_k dµ)(∫ η^z_{m−k} f dµ) is proved with its Fubini and product-integrability steps. When (z,m) is admitted and the logarithm pushes µ forward to c·Lebesgue (0 < c < ∞), the dual memberships η̃^z_k ∈ L²(µ), k ≤ m, are derived and the identity holds for every F ∈ L²(µ) through the representative F/w^{1/2}. Both required tests are stated explicitly: for a Haar measure µ, A(φ#) and A((ℓφ)#) act with coefficients (−1)^k·conj(∫ φ η^{2a−z̄}_k dµ) and (−1)^k·conj(∫ φ η^{2a−z̄}_{k+1} dµ) respectively; in the nuclear run the coefficients are identified as the iterated complex derivatives M^{(i)}_{φ#}(z) and −M^{(i+1)}_{φ#}(z) of the Mellin transform M_h(w) = ∫ h χ e^{wℓ} dµ, for measurable inputs with finitely many integrable jet moments. At operator level, on an abstract Hilbert space carrying a translation family that satisfies the binomial law on a dense set, the integrated operator A = ∫ h T_g dµ, the invariance of the common kernel G of the jets, the induced quotient operator Ā, the factorization P_{G^⊥}A = S Ā Q and the jet action of Ā on the quotient functionals are all derived.

*Trace as a sum over admitted zeros.* Finite level: the lower-triangular binomial matrix of (10.3.1.17) has trace n·a(0) (and −n·c(1) for the (ℓφ)# coefficient pattern); the strict boundary count {j < N: 2j+1 < s} has min(N, ⌈(s−1)/2⌉₊) elements; on the N-dimensional span of log-jets on C_K the transposed convolution (translated-coefficient) operator has trace N·∫ φ c dµ. Infinite level, on an arbitrary complex Hilbert space H with a closed subspace E, its orthogonal projection P and an arbitrary bounded A: if P∘A has a nuclear expansion Σ⟨u_n,·⟩v_n with Σ‖u_n‖‖v_n‖ < ∞, E has a Hilbert basis indexed by (component, zero, jet order below the native multiplicity), and A acts on that basis by a lower flag whose diagonal entry is T(zero), then the multiplicity-weighted zero sum converges absolutely and equals the nuclear trace Σ⟨u_n,v_n⟩, which equals Σ_κ⟨e_κ, PA e_κ⟩ for every Hilbert basis (e_κ) of H. The native multiplicity is the full analytic order strictly inside the strip, #{m < order: 2m+1 < s} on its boundary, and 0 outside. The flag diagonal is derived from a raw triangular jet action through Gram–Schmidt; the actual admitted jets of a fixed χ with distinct (z,m) labels are proved linearly independent in L²(µ) with an orthonormal predecessor flag. Finite window matrices of the triangular action reproduce P_J∘A and converge strongly to P_{G^⊥}∘A, and bounded strongly convergent cutoffs (left, or two-sided) applied to a nuclear expansion of P∘A give convergent cutoff traces with limit Σ⟨u_n,v_n⟩.

<a id="result-7"></a>
## Result 7 — (10.4.2.20); C.3.7.1; 12.1.1.2

1. *Diagonal integral = trace, on the actual objects, at finite rank, for both tests.* For every finite set T of vectors, let E_T be the span of the admitted theta vectors lying in T (a finite-dimensional subspace of the closed theta subspace W = G). The kernel finiteKernel_T, built from the orthonormal basis u_i of E_T and the adjoint rows A*u_i, represents P_{E_T}∘A on every input; its diagonal is μ-integrable and ∫ finiteKernel_T(x,x) dμ = trace(P_{E_T}∘A). The residual kernel h(xy⁻¹) − finiteKernel_T represents (1 − P_{E_T})∘A, which converges strongly to P^spec∘A as T grows; its diagonal equals −finiteKernel_T(x,x) (h(1) = 0 because φ vanishes on norm one) and integrates to −trace(P_{E_T}∘A). Both diagonals also satisfy the physical-band limit, the cutoff-regime limit for every mask equal to 1 on the plateau (Λ → ∞ and εΛ → 0⁺ independently), and the divisorial-shell HasSum. At a fixed mask ρ: ∫ ρ(x) finiteKernel_T(x,x) dμ = trace(P_{E_T}∘A∘M_ρ) and ∫ ρ·finiteKernel_T·ρ = trace(M_ρ∘P_{E_T}∘A∘M_ρ) (rows 1–3). In the AP order, trace(P_{E_T}∘A∘P^spec) = 0 equals the integral of its kernel diagonal, with cutoff limits 0 (row 17).
2. *Diagonal integral = trace for an operator with a supplied nuclear expansion (full operator).* If T F = Σ ⟨u_n, F⟩ v_n for every F with Σ ‖u_n‖‖v_n‖ < ∞, the diagonal of the row kernel Σ_n haarKernel(v_n, u_n) is integrable, its integral is trace T, it is reconstructed by every positive partition, and its masked integrals converge to trace T (sequences, and the cutoff regime on C_K) (rows 4–5). For P^spec∘A: with the Hilbert-basis rows of G^⊥ under Σ_i ‖A*e_i‖ < ∞ (row 5); on the actual objects for both tests, from a supplied nuclear expansion of A∘P^spec (the printed order), one jointly measurable kernel represents P^spec∘A on every input, with integrable diagonal and ∫ k(x,x) dμ = trace(P^spec∘A) (row 6); generically, for an invariant closed subspace S, from a nuclear expansion of the compression of A to S^⊥ (with S = G, the operator the manuscript transports to A^spec_a(φ)), with trace((1 − P_S)∘A) equal to the trace of the compression (row 7). Any absolutely summable rank-one family has an L¹ diagonal density with the scalar sum as integral (row 8).
3. *Cutoff trace limits.* PA order in a general Hilbert space: for a supplied nuclear expansion of P_E∘A and uniformly bounded cutoffs M_k → Id strongly, Σ_i ⟨b_i, M_k P_E A b_i⟩ → Σ ⟨u_n, v_n⟩ = trace(P_E∘A) in every Hilbert basis b; two-sided and iterated versions; trace-class versions trace(M_a∘T) → trace T and trace(T∘P_a) → trace T; right-mask version trace(A∘M_ρ) → trace A (rows 10–13). On the actual objects, both tests, with supplied nuclear expansions of A∘M and P^spec∘A: trace(A M) − ∫ mask·finiteKernel_T → trace(P^spec A M) as T → ∞, trace(P^spec A M_ρ) → trace(P^spec A) along manuscript cutoff sequences and along the cutoff regime, and nuclear-norm convergence of the source approximants (row 14). AP order (printed order of (10.4.2.20)), actual objects, both tests, from a supplied nuclear expansion of A∘P^spec: P^spec∘A has the projected expansion with no larger cost and trace(P^spec A M_ρ) → trace(P^spec A); A∘P − P∘A = S∘A∘P, trace(S A P) = 0, and trace(S A P M_p), trace(M_p S A P) → 0 along the cutoff regime (rows 15–16). Coefficient level: masked integrals of the shifted convolution coefficients converge to the Mellin products, with an explicit L¹ majorant or weighted-L² nuclear bound (row 20).
4. *Smoothening along the diagonal.* On C_K, a decreasing neighbourhood base gives rectangles V_n×V_n shrinking to (x,x); normalized rectangle averages carry a multiplier continuous at (x,x) when the normalized absolute averages are bounded (the step (12.1.1.13) at one diagonal point) (row 18). For the complement kernel φ(xy⁻¹) − kspec(x,y) with φ = 0 on norm one, ∫ ρ_n·(diagonal) dμ → −∫ kspec(x,x) dμ under the manuscript endpoints, given integrability of kspec(x,x), or given approximating diagonals with L¹ convergence, which is derived from bounded-multiplier estimates (row 19).
5. *Conventions.* The half weight cancels on the diagonal, and the diagonal does not depend on the half weight; a 2×2 example shows that a diagonal entry is not determined by trace cyclicity (row 21).

Notation used in the table (all from the Lean sources). C_K = `MasulloGRH.HeckeIdeleClasses K` = 𝔸_K^×/K^× (actual idele class group, any number field K), ∣x∣ = exp(classLog x) the product-formula modulus. μ: a left-invariant measure on C_K (σ-finite, locally finite; inversion-invariant in Main); no normalization imposed. L²(μ) is the half-weight model of W_I^s(C_K) for I = [l, r]: b = `intervalHalfWeight l r s ∘ classLog`, b(x)² = max(∣x∣^{2l}, ∣x∣^{2r})(1 + log²∣x∣)^{s/2} = w_{I,s}. A = `PIntervalConvolution.intervalConvolutionCLM` (Pettis convolution, weighted translations conjugated by the half weight) by h = `LIntegrated.requiredTest K φ q`, q = 0: φ#(x) = conj φ(x⁻¹), q = 1: (ℓφ)#. W = `JThetaAttachment.fullThetaClosure` (closure of the normalized principal theta series of original Schwartz–Bruhat tests with f(0) = 0, ∫f = 0, summable series, theta in L²; contains every `thetaClosure χ`), S = P_W = P^geom, P = 1 − S = P^spec. E_T = `DSourceKernel.sourceSpan … T` (= `fullFiniteSpan … T`). haarKernel_V(U,R)(x,y) = (U(x)/b(x))·(b(y)·conj R(y))/V; finiteKernel_T = Σ_i haarKernel_1(u_i, A*u_i). trace = `Masullo.TraceClass.trace` (Hilbert-basis tsum); for finite rank `HOperatorTrace.finite_rank_hasSum` and for nuclear expansions `HOperatorTrace.nuclear_trace_basis` (Main p. 159) prove absolute HasSum in every Hilbert basis with value Σ⟨u_n, v_n⟩. `FPhysicalFilter.ValidCutoff L e`:= 0 < L ∧ 0 < e ∧ 0 < L⁻¹ − e < L − e (the parameter condition of Definition 9.3.3.1); `cutoffRegime` = atTop ×ˢ 𝓝[>]0 in coordinates (Λ, δ = εΛ). Masks: ρ measurable, 0 ≤ ρ ≤ 1 (or ω a.e.-strongly measurable, ‖ω‖ ≤ 1), equal to 1 on {Λ⁻¹ + ε ≤ ∣x∣ ≤ Λ − ε} for valid (Λ, ε); support in [1/Λ, Λ], symmetry, C_K¹-invariance and smoothness are not required, so every manuscript (Λ, ε)-cutoff is admitted. "Smooth mask" = `FSmoothCutoff.realMask K L ε` = logCutoff(L, ε, log∣x∣): C^∞ in log∣x∣, symmetric under x ↦ x⁻¹, values in [0,1], supported in L⁻¹ ≤ ∣x∣ ≤ L, equal to 1 on the plateau; it is a cutoff mask, not the diagonal smoothening of 12.1.1.1. M_ρ = `KPhysicalMask.realMaskCLM` (multiplication operator, norm ≤ 1).

<a id="result-8"></a>
## Result 8 — Theorem 11.3.2.1, §12.4.1 (Theorem 12.4.1.1)

- Global assembly. `PIntegrated.geometric_trace_global_operator_assembly` (15,152-line source), for every K, a regular add-Haar measure on A_K, a left- and inversion-invariant, σ-finite, locally finite measure µ on C_K, l ≤ r, s ≥ 0, φ continuous with compact support and φ(C^1) = 0, both tests, every sequence v of finite places and every F ∈ L²(µ): P∘A = P∘A∘P (compression to W⊥) and (P∘A)F' = Σ_i⟨A*e_i, F'⟩e_i for a Hilbert basis (e_i) of W⊥; for every finite T the residual kernel represents A F − P_{E_T}(A F) (a.e. and in L²), and these vectors converge to (P∘A)F as T grows; the kernel of P_{E_T}A represents P_{E_T}(A F), its diagonal is integrable with integral tr(P_{E_T}A), the physical-band truncations converge to it, and the descended arithmetic shells of the diagonal sum to tr(P_{E_T}A); the residual diagonal d_T is integrable with ∫ d_T = −tr(P_{E_T}A), its shells sum to −tr(P_{E_T}A) absolutely with Σ_e‖∫ p_e d_T‖ ≤ ∫‖d_T‖, and the band truncations converge shellwise in ℓ¹; for any measurable ω with ‖ω‖ ≤ 1 and ω = 1 on the plateau [L⁻¹ + e, L − e] of a ValidCutoff, ∫ ω·(finite kernel diagonal) → tr(P_{E_T}A) and ∫ ω·d_T → −tr(P_{E_T}A) along cutoffRegime; for measurable cutoffs ρ with values in [0, 1] equal to 1 on that plateau, M_ρ(1 − P_{E_T})A F → (P∘A)F and M_ρ(1 − P_{E_T})A M_ρ F → (P∘A)F along cutoffRegime × (T → ∞); (1 − P_{E_T})A F → P A F; every single-character theta closure lies in W; (P∘A)∘S = 0 and A∘P − P∘A = S∘(A∘P).
- Shell decomposition: descended divisorial masks with the constructed indentation form a measurable positive partition of unity on C_K; reconstruction with ℓ¹ control for every L¹ density; unfolding of each shell from C_K to the ideles with the Haar scalar c; shell decompositions for finite-rank kernels P_E∘A (generic), the actual finite-level kernels (both tests), nuclear-row kernels with supplied nuclear decomposition, and T → ∞ limits of the shell vector of S∘N for a supplied nuclear N; the §12.3-type decomposition with S-idele residual (C13).
- Orbital traces: the tensor-indented orbital diagonal integrates to c·tr(P_E A) (finite rank, generic E, A), to −c·tr(P_{E_T}A) for the actual residual kernel (both tests), to c·tr(P A) for the full operator given a nuclear expansion of A∘P; cutoff limits of these (Issue 31 table); the transport (12.2.2.3)–(12.2.2.7) (Issue 33 table).
- S-local calculation and constants: Issue 34 table (local expressions with 1/2, 1/(2π), log N(v)/µ_v(O_v^×); finite-place terms; φ(1)log(λλ′) = 0; S-local cutoff integrals; periodization (12.4.1.4)).
- Haar normalizations: the indentation quotient measure on C_K equals c·µ for every Haar λ on the ideles and Haar µ on C_K, with c > 0 (value not computed); ∫ b·(F∘mk)dλ = c∫F dµ; ℓ pushes µ to µ{0 ≤ ℓ < 1}·Lebesgue; C_K^1 is compact; Haar measures on C_K are σ-finite and inversion invariant; local Haar measures with µ_v(O_v^×) = 1 exist and give the finite coefficient log q_v; scalar factors propagate through projected traces and must multiply the local terms (scalar statements).

<a id="result-9"></a>
## Result 9 — §1.4.4–1.4.5

- Strip-limit endpoint: for an entire function f, not identically zero, whose zeros satisfy hcount, if for all 0 < a < b and Re z > b the symmetric-height interior sums Σ_{|Re(ρ−1/2)|<a, |Im(ρ−1/2)|≤H} N(ρ)·concreteTransform z n (ρ − 1/2) and the same with b converge (H → ∞) to one common limit, then every zero of f has real part 1/2. Variants: a general test family T with a cutoff-uniform bound B/|ρ − 1/2|² and limit (z − (ρ − 1/2))^{−2}; a nonzero common scale κ; widths restricted to boundary-free widths, or to any set of widths bracketing every positive number; zeros confined to |Re ρ − 1/2| ≤ W with z restricted to Re z > max(b, W); abstract indexed points with resolvent-difference tests.
- The equality of strip limits used there is, under corrected trace limits, equivalent to the conclusion (Issue 35 rows BoundaryFreeTraceRepairD.remainder_independent_iff_critical_line, ActualOmittedD.omitted_resolvent_independent_iff_line, NativeLoggedCriterionD.native_logged_width_independent_iff_line); with full and omitted height limits supplied, equal omitted limits at the two widths give the conclusion (FiniteHeightRepairE.critical_line_of_actual_omitted_height_limits).
- Continuation endpoint (abstract form of §1.4.5): if for every a > 0 there is an open preconnected domain containing 1/2 + a < Re < 1 and a base point with Re > 1 on which f, f′ and a coefficient H_a are analytic, the domain avoids −iτ and 1 − iτ, and f′ = (H_a − δ((z + iτ)^{−1} + (z − 1 + iτ)^{−1}))·f near the base point, then f has no zero of positive order in 1/2 < Re < 1; with the same data for a dual g (twist −τ) and f(z) = ε g(1 − z), ε ≠ 0, on 0 < Re < 1, every zero of f in 0 < Re < 1 has real part 1/2. The coefficient may be given as a local term plus the subtracted resolvent series of window zeros (Re ρ ≤ 1/2 + a, Σ m/(1 + |ρ|²) < ∞), whose holomorphy on Re > 1/2 + a is derived.
- Local log-derivative facts: a nonzero analytic germ vanishing at ρ has no continuous logarithmic-derivative extension at ρ; (z − a)·logDeriv f → order; a continuous corrected logarithmic derivative fixes the signed order (−1 at a listed pole, 0 elsewhere); pole residues of the logarithmic derivative are negative; multiplying by (w − a)(w − b) adds 1/(z−a) + 1/(z−b); L′/L(s) = −b − L_dual′/L_dual(1 − s) from L(z) = e^{a−bz}L_dual(1 − z).
- For the constructed finite Hecke Euler product: L_f is analytic and nonzero on Re s > 1; any two meromorphic continuations of L_f from Re s > 1 agree on punctured neighbourhoods everywhere and have equal orders.
- For K = Q (record interface): under Chapter13RegularizedLimit, the paired correction-control condition is equivalent to Mathlib's RiemannHypothesis; the checked file proves RiemannHypothesis from nonvanishing of ζ on Re s > 1/2, s ≠ 1 (internal lemma riemannHypothesis_of_right_nonvanishing, used by the printed declarations).

<a id="issue-1"></a>
## Issue 1 — §1.4.2–1.4.3, repeated in §1.8.4.3

- For the u = +1 real-place integrand of F = Q, χ = 1 at z = 3/2 (the scalar kernel t^{−2}/(t − 1) on t > 1, written 1/((1+u)²u) with u = t − 1): the kernel is ≥ 1/(4(t − 1)) on (1, 2]; ∫_{1+ε}^{2} ≥ −log(ε)/4 for 0 < ε ≤ 1; for every M there is ε ∈ (0, 1] with ∫_{1+ε}^{2} > M; the truncated integrals tend to +∞ as ε → 0+. These are statements about truncated integrals of the kernel over [1+ε, 2]; several versions re-check them.
- Repairs at the real place: (i) reference subtraction at fixed parameters (kernel at z = 3/2 minus kernel at z = 1/2 = −1/t²; at z = 3/2 minus z = 5/2 = 1/t³, and for every continuous cutoff h with values in [0,1] the corresponding integral ∫_ε^1 h(u)/(1+u)³ du lies in [0,1]); (ii) a counterterm: kernel(1+x) − 1/x = −(2+x)/(1+x)², continuous at 0 with value −2 and bounded by 2 on x ≥ 0; (iii) for all complex parameters, (e^{au} − e^{bu})/(e^u − 1) → a − b and, for q ≠ 0, (e^{−zx} − e^{−wx})/(1 − e^{−qx}) → (w − z)/q as the variable → 0.
- The reference-subtracted family in log coordinates x = log t, logTest N z w x = logCutoff N x · (e^{−zx} − e^{−wx}) with logCutoff N vanishing for x ≤ 1/N and x ≥ N + 1 and equal to 1 on [2/N, N]: for the positive branch density e^{x/2}·logTest/(e^x − 1) there is a cutoff-independent bound e^{1/2}e^R‖z − w‖ on 0 < x ≤ 1 for ‖z‖, ‖w‖ ≤ R; the whole integrals over x > 0 converge, as N = n + 1 → ∞, uniformly on {‖z‖, ‖w‖ ≤ R, Re z, Re w ≥ σ} for every σ > −1/2; the limit integral is holomorphic in z on Re z > −1/2 (fixed w with Re w > −1/2). The same holds for the negative branch with denominator e^x + 1 (see Issue 2).
- Complex place: for normalized Haar measure on the circle and r > 1, ∫ |1 − ru|^{−2} dμ(u) = 1/(r² − 1), and |∫ χ(u)|1 − ru|^{−2} dμ| ≤ 1/(r² − 1) for every profile with |χ| ≤ 1; angular series/integral interchange for |1 − ru|^{−2}.
- Zero side of the Montel paragraph (abstract or Q): cutoff-uniform inverse-square bound and pointwise limit (z − w)^{−2} for the concrete logged transform; holomorphy of subtracted-resolvent zero series on window-separated half-planes; for K = Q, a family converging pointwise to the logged correction near an omitted zero admits no cutoff-independent local bound.
- Mollifier: shrinking normalized kernels η_j have ∫ η_j(u)e^{zu} du → 1 uniformly on ‖z‖ ≤ R.

<a id="issue-2"></a>
## Issue 2 — §1.4.3

- The u = −1 real-place branch is defined with denominator e^x + 1 = |1 + t| (t = e^x), the form |1 − ut| at u = −1. For the reference-subtracted test: the branch density is integrable on x > 0 for Re z, Re w > −1/2; differentiation under the integral holds and the limit integral is holomorphic in z on Re z > −1/2; the cutoff integrals converge for each pair and uniformly on {‖z‖, ‖w‖ ≤ R, Re z, Re w ≥ σ}, σ > −1/2; the denominator is dominated by the u = +1 denominator (|a/(e^x + 1)| ≤ |a/(e^x − 1)| for x > 0), and the cutoff error is dominated by the u = +1 error.
- On the geometric side, the two real signs combine with denominators 1 − r and 1 + r: (1/(1−r) ± 1/(1+r))/2 = 1/(1−r²) or r/(1−r²), and (1/(r−1) ± 1/(r+1))/2 = r/(r²−1) or 1/(r²−1).

## Issue 3 — §1.4.4

- For every number field K, every unitary Hecke character χ (continuous Circle-valued on the constructed ideles, trivial on principal ideles, no norm-twist restriction) and the constructed finite Euler product L_f = finiteHeckeL K χ: with genuine prime-power terms log(N v)·(χ_v(π_v)N v^{−s})^{m+1} at unramified v (0 at ramified v), and scalar cutoff weights c_n(v, m) with |c_n| ≤ 1 and c_n → 1 pointwise, the cutoff sums converge to −L′_f/L_f(s) for Re s > 1, and uniformly over all characters and all s with Re s ≥ σ > 1 when c_n does not depend on (χ, s). With s = z + 1/2 this is the sum of 1.4.4, with the minus sign.
- Other versions state the same with the minus sign inside the term (heckePrimePowerLogTerm = −(χ_v(π_v)N v^{−s})^{n+1}·log N v), converging to +L′_f/L_f; for real bumps h_j(t) ∈ [0,1] on t > 1 with h_j → 1, weights h_j(N v^{n+1}) give uniform convergence on Re s ≥ σ > 1.
- Local sign: −(d/ds) log (1 − c e^{−sℓ})^{−1} = ℓ c e^{−sℓ}/(1 − c e^{−sℓ}) = Σ_{n≥0} ℓ (c e^{−sℓ})^{n+1}.
- Mollifier at prime powers: for ε < log 2 and η vanishing outside [−ε, ε], the logarithmic convolution of φ_z (= e^{−zx} on x > 0) with η at x = log N v^{n+1} equals N v^{−(n+1)z}·∫ η(u)e^{zu} du, with error ≤ |N v^{−(n+1)z}|·2Rε for ‖z‖ ≤ R, ∫η = 1, ∫‖η‖ ≤ 1, Rε < 1.
- For K = Q, χ = 1: Σ Λ(n)n^{−(z+1/2)} = −ζ′/ζ(z + 1/2) for Re z > 1/2; smooth cutoffs positiveCutoff(n+1, log k) converge uniformly on Re s ≥ σ > 1 to −ζ′/ζ for the plain family and to +(ζ′/ζ)′ for the logged family.

<a id="issue-4"></a>
## Issue 4 — Theorem 2.2.3.4, (2.2.3.10)–(2.2.3.12), with (A.2.2.11)

Printed: the proof chooses τ = s_{F,δ}, whose archimedean component at v is t^{1/} (N_v = 1 real, 2 complex; A.2.2.11), and then uses ‖t_∞ξE_∞‖ ≥ A‖t_∞ξ‖ = A|t|_F‖ξ‖ (2.2.3.10) and ‖ξt_∞g_{0,∞}‖^{−N′} = t_0^{−N′}‖ξg_{0,∞}‖^{−N′} (2.2.3.12), with N′ = 1 + max{r1+2r2, N}, to reach Σ_{ξ∈I_f−0}|f_∞(ξt_∞g_{0,∞})| ≤ C_N|g|_F^{−N} (2.2.3.13), i.e. (2.2.3.4): |Φ_F(f)(g)| ≤ C_N|g|_F^{−N} as |g|_F → ∞.

- With the manuscript's place-dependent exponents 1/(N_i·r), N_i ∈ {1,2}, r = number of places: for every finite product of normed fields ∏F_i (each finite-dimensional over ℝ), every compact set S of coordinatewise invertible archimedean multipliers, every discrete ℤ-lattice L, every Schwartz function f, every family of coefficients w(t,y,x) bounded by V, and every rate B, there is P ≥ 0 with |Σ_{x∈L−0} w(t,y,x) f((e^{t/(N_i r)}y_i x_i)_i)| ≤ P e^{−Bt} for all t ≥ 0 and y ∈ S (Round197F.diagonal_norm_section_theta_decay). With t = log t_0 the scaling e^{t/(N_i r)} is t_0^{1/}, the archimedean component of s_{F,δ}(t_0), so this is the bound (2.2.3.13) in t_0 = |s_{F,δ}(t_0)|_F, of every order, along the manuscript's section. The proof uses the slowest exponent δ = 1/(2r) (Round196F.diagonal_section_coercive: e^{δt}‖x‖ ≤ ‖(e^{q_i t}x_i)_i‖ for δ ≤ q_i, t ≥ 0) and a Schwartz order n > max{rank L, B/δ}. The constant A of (2.2.3.10) is derived from compactness of S (Round197F.compact_archimedean_multiplier_coercive). Lattice summability is derived (Mathlib ZLattice.summable_norm_pow_inv), not assumed. These statements are about an abstract product, lattice, compact set and seed given as parameters.
- On the actual objects, for every number field K, with the isotropic dilation x ↦ t·x of the mixed space K_ℝ = ℝ^{r1}×ℂ^{r2} (the same real factor t at every archimedean place; along the log section t = e^{u/[K:Q]}, which is the section s_F of (A.2.2.8), not s_{F,δ}):
- for every invertible fractional ideal I and Schwartz f on K_ℝ, Σ_{x∈I−0}|f(t·x)| ≤ C t^{−N} for t ≥ 1, every N (FollowupA636.actual_ideal_dilation_decay), with C = C_N·p_{k,0}(f) uniform in f (FollowupB639.actual_ideal_dilation_uniform);
- for every original Schwartz–Bruhat test f on A_K (finite sum of Schwartz(K_ℝ) ⊗ locally constant compactly supported finite-adelic functions) and every compact family U of ideles, |Φ_K(f)(radial(t)·a)| ≤ B t^{−N} for t ≥ 1, a ∈ U, every N, where Φ_K(f)(x) = Σ_{u∈K^×} f(ux) is the actual adelic theta series on C_K (JThetaAttachment.originalBruhat_theta_radial_bound), equivalently |Φ_K(f)(s_F(e^u)a)| ≤ B e^{−Ru} for u ≥ 0, every R (originalBruhat_theta_log_bound);
- on the whole half {x ∈ C_K: log|x| ≥ 0}: |w_{α,s}^{1/2}(log|x|) e^{a·log|x|} Φ_K(f)(x)| ≤ B e^{−rate·log|x|} for every rate (CThetaLogDecay.originalBruhat_normalizedTheta_global_positive_decay). With a = α = s = 0 this is (2.2.3.4) for every number field and every original Schwartz–Bruhat test.
- Two-sided decay (Theorem 2.2.3.4(3)) on the actual C_K from forward decay of Φ_K(f) and Φ_K(f̂) and the Poisson identity, both supplied as hypotheses (FollowupA635.actual_theta_two_sided_decay); in the abstract lattice model, two-sided decay from the lattice representation and the zero-mass Poisson identity supplied as hypotheses (Round196F.theta_decay_of_lattice_poisson).

## Issue 5 — (3.4.3.15), Remark 3.4.3.4

The identity Z = L·Δ near a zero ρ enters every checked converse theorem as an explicit hypothesis (`hdiv`), assumed for the tests in a dense submodule D (the factorized tests); from it, with L analytic of finite order at ρ and the quotient analytic, the m-th derivative of Z at ρ vanishes for m < ord_ρ L, and the vanishing is then extended to all tests of S₀ by density and continuity. The local archimedean pieces of the repair derivation are checked separately: the real Gaussian integrals equal Γ_ℝ(z) and Γ_ℝ(z+1); at a complex place the Taylor polar terms divided by Γ are entire (for every angular mode m and real twist τ); uniform Schwartz-seminorm bounds hold for complex Taylor remainders, for angular averages uniformly in the mode, and for the full mixed archimedean Schwartz space (no pure-tensor hypothesis). At finite places, the unramified Euler factor is the sum of its valuation-ray series and each unramified local shell integral is the corresponding series term.

<a id="issue-6"></a>
## Issue 6 — (6.3.2.8); proof of Theorem 7.1.4.2

The checked shifted pole-clearing statements use the polynomial (z+iτ)(z−(1−iτ)) = (z+iτ)(z−1+iτ) (Round19A/20A: (z+iτ)(z+iτ−k) for a WeakFEPair of weight k), whose roots are −iτ and 1−iτ, the poles of L(s,∣·∣^{iτ}) = ζ_K(s+iτ). In the notation of (6.3.2.8), where L(s,χ) = ζ̂_F(s+it₀) with residues at −it₀ and 1−it₀, this is τ = t₀ and the polynomial is −(s+it₀)(1−s−it₀); in the notation of 7.1.4.2(1) it is (s+ix₀)(s−1+ix₀) with τ = x₀. The polynomial is entire and nonzero on Re z > 1, its divisor is [x = −iτ] + [x = 1−iτ], dividing by it adds −1 exactly at those two points, and with the WeakFEPair constants nonzero the cleared function is nonzero at both points. For K = ℚ the corresponding cleared completed zeta function equals 1 at both shifted poles.

| Declaration | Proves | Objects | Scope | Hypotheses |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| `MasulloGRH.Round6A.normTwist_clearingFactor_entire_and_ne_zero` | entire; ≠ 0 for Re z > 1 | polynomial | every τ | none |
| `Round19A.shifted_pole_clearing_identification` | G(z) = (z+iτ)(z+iτ−k)Λ₀(z+iτ) − (z+iτ−k)f₀ + (z+iτ)εg₀ everywhere; G(−iτ) = k f₀; G(k−iτ) = kεg₀ | Mathlib WeakFEPair P (weight k), supplied entire G | every τ | G differentiable; G = (z+iτ)(z+iτ−k)·mellin(f−f₀)(z+iτ) on Re z > k |
| `MasulloGRH.rationalHeckeXi_at_pole`, `rationalHeckeXi_at_other_pole` | Ξ_τ(−iτ) = 1, Ξ_τ(1−iτ) = 1 | Ξ_τ = (s+iτ)(s+iτ−1)·completedRiemannZeta₀(s+iτ) + 1 | K = ℚ | none |

## Issue 7 — Theorem 7.1.4.2(1), (7.1.4.23)

No checked declaration states a topology on Z_χ, on O(C)/ΛO(C), or on its continuous dual; none states that a continuous functional on an infinite product of finite-dimensional spaces factors through finitely many coordinates, nor an isomorphism with a product or a direct sum. The individual-jet results, which do not depend on the product/direct-sum claim, are checked (Result 4): each strictly admitted jet of order below the finite analytic order annihilates the closed theta span, under local Tate division and the other displayed hypotheses.

## Issue 8 — Lemma 9.3.1.5, (9.3.1.6)

Printed (p. 421): for G a second-countable ℝ_{>0}-modulated group, I = [a,b], s ≥ 0, every continuous seminorm p on L_β(W_I^s(G)) satisfies p(ρ_G(g,·)) ≤ C_p max{|g|^a,|g|^b}|log|g||^{s/2}; p(f) is µ_G-measurable; for I = {b} the operator norm grows at most polynomially. The proof takes p = operator norm and ends with (9.3.1.7), which keeps additive constants.

- The printed right side of (9.3.1.6) cannot bound ρ(1) = Id: for every C and every s > 0, the scalar inequality 1 ≤ C·e^0·|0|^{s/2} is false (Round92C), and for every C, α the inequality 1 ≤ C·max{1^α,1^{−α}}·|log 1| is false (Round88F, the s = 2 case at g = 1).
- The corrected bound, with the additive constant, is proved as an operator-norm bound for the actual left translation on the weighted L² space over the actual idele class group C_K, for every number field K, I = [−α,α] with α ≥ 0, every s ≥ 0, and every left-invariant measure µ: ‖ρ(g)‖ ≤ e^{α|log|g||}·(2(1+(log|g|)²))^{s/4} = 2^{s/4} max{|g|^{−α},|g|^{α}} (1+(log|g|)²)^{s/4} (SpectralCycle8G.actualTranslation_bound; the operator SpectralCycle8G.actualTranslationCLM is built from this bound). On the same space ρ(gh) = ρ(g)ρ(h) and ρ(1) = Id (ConditionalC880).
- For a general interval I = [u,v] (not necessarily symmetric), on the log-coordinate fiber L²(ℝ, w_{I,s}(x)^{−1}dx) (w_{I,s}(x) = max{e^{2ux},e^{2vx}}(1+x²)^{s/2}, Lebesgue measure), translation f ↦ f(·+t) is a bounded operator with ‖U(t)‖ ≤ e^{(max{|u|,|v|}+s/4)|t|}, U(0) = Id, U(t)U(r) = U(r+t), and t ↦ U(t)f is continuous for every f (Round195D, Round196D). The bound equals 1 at t = 0.
- Pointwise Peetre-type weight inequalities w(x+t) ≤ c(t)·w(x) with c(0) ≥ 1 are proved in several normalizations, together with the integrated weighted-norm versions over an arbitrary measure space.
- The measurability clause: g ↦ ‖ρ(g)‖ is measurable on C_K (ConditionalC882), with weak/strong measurability of the orbits g ↦ ρ(g)F (ConditionalD881/D882, SpectralA905), under explicit measurability/separability instance hypotheses.
- Downstream use in Lemma 9.3.2.1 (p. 423): the corrected bound is integrated against continuous compactly supported tests h on C_K, giving ‖∫h(g)ρ(g)F dµ‖ ≤ (∫|h(g)|·e^{α|log|g||}(2(1+log²|g|))^{s/4} dµ)·‖F‖ and a bounded convolution operator (ConditionalD880, FollowupD636).

## Issue 9 — Lemma 9.3.2.4

For K = ℚ, every Dirichlet character χ mod N and every real norm twist τ, Jensen's formula is proved for the entire pole-cleared function s ↦ L(s+iτ, χ) (with (s+iτ−1)L for the principal character), the function satisfies ‖F(c)‖ ≥ 1/dirichletCenterMajorant at every centre with Re c ≥ 2 (dirichletCenterMajorant = 1 + Σ_n n^{−2}), and the total multiplicity of zeros of the completed function χ.completedLFunction(s+iτ) in any unit-height band of the open critical strip is bounded by (circle mean of log‖pole-cleared function‖ on the circle of radius 11/4 about 2+iT, plus log of an explicit constant) divided by log(11/10). The polynomial growth bound on that circle, which would give O(log T), is not proved (docstrings: "The circle growth estimate remains unproved", "a polynomial circle growth estimate is still required"). A separate checked step turns a weighted count Σ μ_i ≤ C·T(1+log T) into Σ μ_i/h_i² < ∞. No declaration is about M(φ) for a general φ ∈ S_E(G).

## Issue 10 — Theorem 9.3.2.6, (9.3.2.10)

For an arbitrary family of functionals indexed by height, the joint kernels {v: e_i v = 0 for all i of height ≤ T} decrease as T increases. For a continuous linear functional e on a normed space and any sequence of maps P_n with e(P_n v) = 0 for n ≥ N and e v ≠ 0, P_n v does not converge to v (docstring cites 9.3.2.6). In a Hilbert space, the orthogonal complement of the closed span of vectors r_i equals the joint kernel of the functionals ⟨r_i, ·⟩ (docstring: "rather than intersections of an increasing family of jet kernels (manuscript p. 426)"), and the projections onto spans of finite subfamilies converge strongly, along finite sets, to the projection onto the closed span; for an increasing directed family of closed subspaces dense in E, the projections converge strongly to the projection onto E. Finite linear independence of the actual normalized Mellin-jet vectors across distinct exponents, orders and compact-character components is proved on C_K (see Result 4 and Issue 12).

## Issue 11 — (10.2.1.6), (10.2.2.5), (10.3.1.4), (10.3.2.4), (11.2.0.6)

The jet density x^{2m}(1+x²)^{−s/2} is integrable on (1,∞) iff 2m+1 < s (s ≥ 0), and the boundary density e^{2βx−2α∣x∣}x^{2m}(1+x²)^{−s/2} with ∣β∣ = α ≥ 0 is integrable on ℝ iff 2m+1 < s (α = 0 included). On the actual idele class group C_K of any number field, for any unitary Hecke character and any measure mu whose classLog-pushforward is a nonzero finite multiple of Lebesgue measure, the normalized Mellin jet at ∣Re z − a∣ = α lies in L²(mu) iff 2m+1 < s, and the set of admitted orders m < N is {0, …, min(N, ⌈(s−1)/2⌉) − 1}. The finite count {j < N: 2j+1 < s} equals min(N, ⌈(s−1)/2⌉) for every real s, equals min(N, k) at s = 2k+1, and is 0 at s = 1; ¬(2j+1 < 2j+1) is recorded with the docstring "This strict inequality is the native endpoint p-test, not the printed floor formula. At the equality exponent the leading tail is 1/|x|."

<a id="issue-12"></a>
## Issue 12 — Proof of Theorem 10.2.1.1

On the actual C_K, the conjugated normalized Mellin-jet vectors in L²(mu) are linearly independent across all compact-character components and all distinct (exponent, order) labels; distinct components are orthogonal; Gram–Schmidt applied to an ordered jet family yields a predecessor flag with the same finite spans, orthonormal when the family is linearly independent. For finite families of functionals and vectors with invertible pairing matrix, an explicit biorthogonal system is constructed. A quotient-totality criterion is proved: the functionals induced on H/G separate points iff their joint kernel is G.

## Issue 13 — Proposition 10.2.2.2, (10.2.2.8)

- Exact norm density on the actual objects. For every number field K, every unitary Hecke character χ, every z = b+it and every m, the squared W_I^{−s} density of the jet η_{χ,z,m}(x) = χ(x)e^{z·log|x|}(log|x|)^m on the idele class group, with the manuscript weight w_{I,s} = e^{2α|u|}(1+u²)^{s/2} (u = log|x|), is pointwise equal to jetDensity(α,b,s,m;u) = e^{2bu−2α|u|}u^{2m}(1+u²)^{−s/2}; and for every left-invariant, compact-finite, nonzero measure μ on C_K, log|·| pushes μ forward to V·Lebesgue with V = μ(log|·|^{−1}(0,1]) > 0.
- Upper bounds with explicit dependence on m and on the distance δ to the strip boundary, on the actual objects: for |b|+δ ≤ α, δ > 0, s ≥ 0, ‖η_{χ,z,m}‖²_{I,−s} ≤ V·2(2m)!/(2δ)^{2m+1}; for |b| ≤ α and 2m+2 ≤ s, ‖η_{χ,z,m}‖²_{I,−s} ≤ V·π. These bounds do not depend on χ or on t = Im z (V depends on μ). The real-s versions are accepted by Lean (linter warnings only); the even-integer versions s = 2d are checked.
- Scalar (log-coordinate) lower bound and non-uniformity: for α ≥ 0, b ≥ −α, s ≥ 0, ∫_2^3 jetDensity(α,b,s,m;u)du ≥ e^{−12α}4^m/10^{s/2} for every m, and there is no constant C with ∫_2^3 jetDensity(α,b,s,m;u)du ≤ C for all m. At b = 0 the full-line integral satisfies ∫ jetDensity ≥ 4^m e^{−6α}/10^{s/2}.
- Boundary threshold: at |b| = α the density is integrable iff 2m+1 < s (half-line model x^{2m}(1+x²)^{−s/2} on (1,∞); full-line version and L²(μ)-membership version under a Haar-pushforward hypothesis); at s = 2m+1 the tail diverges.

<a id="issue-14"></a>
## Issue 14 — Theorem 10.3.1.1, proof (p. 451)

- *Lower bounds for biorthogonal products (general normed spaces).* Every pair with η(u) = 1 satisfies ‖u‖·‖η‖ ≥ 1, and a finite family with η_i(u_i) = 1 satisfies Σ_{i∈J} ‖u_i‖‖η_i‖ ≥ |J|. Equivalently, ‖ψ‖ ≥ 1/‖η‖: boundedness of the η gives a lower bound on the biorthogonal vectors.
- *Rescaling (general).* For c ≠ 0 the pair (c·u, c⁻¹·η) gives the same rank-one operator η ⊗ A u and the same cost ‖A u‖‖η‖.
- *Separation (general).* If η(v) = 1 and θ(v) = 0, then ‖η − θ‖·‖v‖ ≥ 1; a bound ‖v‖ ≤ C forces ‖η − θ‖ ≥ 1/C; if ‖η − θ‖ ≤ ε‖η‖ then ε·‖v‖‖η‖ ≥ 1.
- *Hilbert form of the biorthogonal vector (general).* With r_i the residual of v_i against the closed span of the other v_j, the vector ‖r_i‖⁻² r_i pairs to 1 with v_i, annihilates the others, has norm ‖r_i‖⁻¹, and is the minimal-norm such vector; ‖r_i‖ ≤ ‖v_i − v_j‖ for every j ≠ i, so 1 ≤ ‖v_i − v_j‖·‖ψ_i‖.
- *The same on the actual weighted idele-class objects.* For every number field K, every nonzero left-invariant measure μ on the idele class group finite on compacts, every family of unitary Hecke characters χ_i, exponents z_i with |Re z_i| ≤ α and jet orders m_i < d (Sobolev index s = 2d), the Riesz vectors v_i of the normalized genuine jets η_i/√w exist in L²(μ) and the residual of v_i against the others is bounded by the L²(μ) distance ‖(η_i − η_j)/√w‖ for every j ≠ i; the single-label statements add ‖v‖² ≤ V·π (V = μ(log⁻¹(0,1]) > 0) and the exact orthogonal-lift cost identity.
- *Dual coefficient differences (log coordinates).* |e^{bu}u^m e^{itu} − e^{bu}u^m e^{izu}| ≤ 2|t − z| e^{bu}|u|^{m+1}; the weighted difference energy is ≤ 4|t − z|² times the next-jet energy (any α, s, b, m; next-jet integrability as hypothesis); for m = 0 in the strip interior (|b| + δ ≤ α, s ≥ 0, δ > 0) the difference density has integral ≤ 2(t − v)²/δ³.
- *Label-dependent finite costs replacing C.* For one spectral block, S∘(block)∘Q has the explicit rank-one representation with first-power cost Σ_{m<n} Σ_{i≤m} binom(m,i)|c_i|‖η_{m−i}‖‖ψ_m‖ (abstract quotient H/G with orthogonal section S); for both tests φ# and (ℓφ)# on the actual idele class group the Mellin coefficients are bounded through one support radius R with all ‖η‖, ‖ψ‖ retained. Inverse-matrix costs use |det|⁻¹·adjugate, with det = (∏ i!)·g(0)^n for exact-order germs; whole finite windows (several roots, cross-root germ relations) have biorthogonal projected representatives with inverse-column costs; projected interpolants have least norm.
- *Cancellation and block estimates without individual products.* Scalar two-root Gram identities for the s = 0 weight e^{2α|x|} give a gap-free two-root cost; abstract resolvent energy inequalities bound Σ_j ‖R e_j‖² by Σ_j Re⟨R e_j, e_j⟩/(z − ω) over finite orthonormal families, with no biorthogonal or inverse-Gram norms (native finite-level application: see Result 5).
- *Independence.* The original-weight native Riesz family is linearly independent across all compact-character components and injective (component, exponent, order) labels, so finite biorthogonal systems exist.

<a id="issue-15"></a>
## Issue 15 — (10.3.1.22)

- Finite block in rank-one form, all indices included. For the block A_ρ = Σ_{m<n}(Σ_{i≤m}C(m,i)c_iη_{m−i})⊗ψ_m on a quotient H/G, lifted to H by the quotient map Q and the orthogonal representative S: S∘A_ρ∘Q equals the explicit sum of n rank-one operators, and its representation cost satisfies Σ_{m=0}^{n−1}‖jetAction_m∘Q‖‖Sψ_m‖ ≤ blockCost = Σ_{m=0}^{n−1}Σ_{i≤m}C(m,i)|c_i|‖η_{m−i}‖‖ψ_m‖ < ∞. Every index m from 0 to n−1 enters the cost. The operator norm of a finite block is bounded by its representation cost.
- The matrix of (10.3.1.17). For a lower-triangular binomial matrix M_{ij} = C(i,j)a_{i−j} (j ≤ i), 0 above the diagonal, trace M = n·a₀; on C_K (every number field) the measured action of a compactly supported h on the jets is ∫h(g)η_{χ,z,a,m}(gy)dμ(g) = Σ_{k≤m}C(m,k)∂^kM(h)(z)·η_{χ,z,a,m−k}(y), which is that binomial lower-triangular form with diagonal coefficient ∂⁰M(h)(z) = M(h)(z).
- Triangular jet pairing matrices. If δ_i(u_j) = 0 for i < j then det(δ_i(u_j)) = ∏_iδ_i(u_i); with diagonal i!·c, c ≠ 0, the determinant is a unit; for residual-germ jets u_j ↔ z^j g(z), det = (∏_i i!)·g(0)^n. These concern the jet pairing (biorthogonalization) matrix δ_i(u_j).
- The K-limit at p. 451. For a fixed finite block and orthogonal (or self-adjoint) cutoffs P_j converging strongly, block∘P_j − block has an explicit rank-one representation whose cost tends to 0, and the operator-norm error tends to 0. All rank-one terms of the block enter.

<a id="issue-16"></a>
## Issue 16 — Theorem 10.3.2.1

- Parameter range. Across the checked statements the hypotheses on the Sobolev degree are 0 ≤ s and jet-order inequalities linking s to the jet order (2m+1 < s for admission, 2m+2 ≤ s in the boundary energy bounds); a search of the statements finds no hypothesis s > 1 on the Sobolev degree. For s ∈ [0,1] and α > 0 the checked statements give: interior jets (|b| < α, or |b|+δ ≤ α) lie in W_I^{−s} for every m with explicit norm bounds; boundary jets (|b| = α) lie in W_I^{−s} iff 2m+1 < s, so for s ≤ 1 there are none; the number of admitted boundary orders below N is #{m < N: 2m+1 < s} = min(N, ⌈(s−1)/2⌉). The measured jet action on L²(μ) and the finite-level nuclear-norm bound are stated for all s ≥ 0.
- Strip versus line. The coefficient bounds and jet-action identities are stated on the closed strip |Re z − a| ≤ α with α ≥ 0 (α = 0 is the line case), and the trace regrouping over zeros uses the strip multiplicity: full analytic order for |Re z − c| < a, the admitted count #{m < N: 2m+1 < s} for |Re z − c| = a, and 0 outside. The factorization P∘A = S∘Ā∘Q and the transfer of a quotient expansion of Ā to an expansion of P∘A (with cost not increasing) are stated for an arbitrary closed A-invariant subspace G of an arbitrary complex Hilbert space, so they do not depend on whether I = {0} or I = [−α,α].
- Schatten class. Every checked operator-cost statement here is a first-power (nuclear, S₁) cost Σ‖f_i‖‖v_i‖ of an explicit rank-one representation, per finite block and for the assembled finite-level operator (`nuclearNorm` = inf of such costs). No checked declaration states an S_p bound with p > 1, or derives S₁ convergence from Σ X^p < ∞.

<a id="issue-17"></a>
## Issue 17 — (10.3.2.8)–(10.3.2.11)

- Mellin derivatives on the actual idele class group, all jet orders, closed strip. For every number field K, every continuous h on C_K with compact support, every continuous χ with |χ| = 1 (unitary Hecke characters included) and every measure μ finite on compacts: z ↦ ∫ hχe^{z·log|x|}dμ is differentiable, its m-th derivative is the log-moment ∫ hχe^{z·log|x|}(log|x|)^m dμ, and one radius R ≥ 1 (from supp h) gives |∂^m M(h)(z)| ≤ e^{αR}R^m‖h‖_{L¹(μ)} for all α ≥ 0, |Re z| ≤ α and all m ∈ ℕ. The factor R^m is kept; these statements carry no decay in |z|.
- Both tests. With h = φ# and h = (ℓφ)# = −ℓ·φ# (φ continuous, compact support), the same R gives |∂^m M(φ#)(z)| ≤ e^{αR}R^m‖φ#‖₁ and |∂^m M((ℓφ)#)(z)| ≤ e^{αR}R^{m+1}‖φ#‖₁, and ∂^m M((ℓφ)#) = −∂^{m+1}M(φ#).
- Height decay with variable jet order, in the logarithmic coordinate. For f ∈ C_c^∞(ℝ) there is R ≥ 1 such that for all m, n ∈ ℕ, α ≥ 0 and |Re z| ≤ α:
|z|^n |∫ x^m f(x)e^{zx}dx| ≤ e^{αR} Σ_{i≤n} C(n,i)·m(m−1)…(m−i+1)·R^{m−i}∫|f^{(n−i)}| ≤ e^{αR}R^m(m+1)^n Σ_{i≤n} C(n,i)∫|f^{(n−i)}|.
This is the estimate with |log t|^{i−n} retained as R^{m−i}: no condition m ≥ n, the test seminorm independent of m, and polynomial growth (m+1)^n in place of a product printed as C·N·O(log|τ|).
- The same estimates applied to finite projected jet blocks (log shift r = 0 for φ#, r = 1 for (ℓφ)#): |z|^N Σ_{m<n}‖jetAction_m∘Q‖‖Sψ_m‖ ≤ e^{αR}R^{n+r}(n+r+1)^N D_N(f)·conditionedCost(η,ψ; 1).

<a id="issue-18"></a>
## Issue 18 — Lemma 10.4.1.2

For an arbitrary complex inner-product space H and a submodule G admitting an orthogonal projection, with Q: H → H/G the quotient map (quotient norm) and S: H/G → H the descent of P_{G^⊥} through Q (S[x] = P_{G^⊥}x, well defined because P_{G^⊥} vanishes on G): ‖S q‖ ≤ ‖q‖, obtained from the infimum defining the quotient norm together with ‖P_{G^⊥}y‖ ≤ ‖y‖ for every representative y; ‖Q‖ ≤ 1 and ‖S‖ ≤ 1; Q(S q) = q; ‖S q‖ = ‖q‖ for every q (from ‖Q v‖ ≤ ‖v‖ and Q∘S = id); range S = G^⊥. The same facts are proved a second time with S typed as a map H/G → G^⊥ (Round173H). On the dual side, pulling a bounded functional back along Q preserves its norm, ‖f∘Q‖ = ‖f‖. The distance-formula inequality ‖v − P_M v‖ ≤ ‖v − q‖ (q ∈ M) and a minimal-norm statement for finite jet windows are also checked. No open-mapping argument enters any of these proofs. All statements are about an abstract H and G given as parameters; no declaration instantiates them with W_I^s(C_F) and G = W^s_{a,I}H_{0,cont}(F^×, S_0(A_F)).

<a id="issue-19"></a>
## Issue 19 — Lemma 10.4.1.2, proof

In the abstract setting of Issue 18 (complex inner-product space H, submodule G with orthogonal projection, quotient map Q, S = descent of P_{G^⊥} through Q): S is defined as `G.liftQ` of P_{G^⊥}, and the condition G ≤ ker P_{G^⊥} is discharged inside the definition; this is the independence of S from the lift ψ̃ of ψ. S(Q x) = P_{G^⊥}x for every x ∈ H (definitional). Surjectivity onto G^⊥ is proved as range S = G^⊥, from S(Q y) = P_{G^⊥}y = y for y ∈ G^⊥, i.e. from S∘Q|_{G^⊥} = id. Further: Q∘S = id_{H/G}, S is injective, S takes values in G^⊥. The same descent construction appears for Bochner convolutions with a G-invariant family (Round173B) and, for finite jet windows, uniqueness of the orthogonal representative determined by its jet data (Round179F). All statements are for abstract H and G; no declaration instantiates them with W_I^s(C_F) and W^s_{a,I}H_{0,cont}(F^×, S_0(A_F)).

<a id="issue-20"></a>
## Issue 20 — (10.4.2.8)

Every checked factorisation is stated with S: H/G → H (or → G^⊥), the orthogonal-representative map, on the left: for bounded A with A(G) ⊆ G, P_{G^⊥}(A x) = S(Ā(Q x)) for all x (definitional) and P_{G^⊥}∘A = S∘Ā∘Q with ‖Q‖ ≤ 1, ‖S‖ ≤ 1 and S isometric. A typed version states that S∘Q = P and Q∘A = B∘Q imply P∘A = S∘B∘Q. For A defined as a Bochner convolution ∫ h(g) T_g dμ(g) over the idele class group HeckeIdeleClasses K of any number field K, with a supplied G-invariant family T bounded by √translationCost, the operator A is constructed, ‖A‖ ≤ ∫|h|·√translationCost dμ, A(G) ⊆ G, Ā∘Q = Q∘A, P_{G^⊥}∘A = S∘Ā∘Q, and nuclearRepresentationCost(P_{G^⊥}∘A) ≤ nuclearRepresentationCost(Ā). The two auxiliary steps (10.4.2.6)–(10.4.2.7) are checked: G is invariant under Bochner convolution, and P(A(P x)) = P(A x). A pointwise series form transfers an expansion of Ā to P∘A. No declaration contains an inverse of S. H and G are parameters throughout; the native weighted space, theta closure and translation action are not attached.

<a id="issue-21"></a>
## Issue 21 — Theorem 10.4.2.8, (10.4.2.21)–(10.4.2.34)

- *(a) Lift norms (10.4.2.24).* Every lift ψ̃ with η(ψ̃) = 1 has ‖ψ̃‖ ≥ 1/‖η‖ (lower half of (10.4.2.24), general normed spaces). A lift that is annihilated by a second coefficient θ satisfies ‖ψ̃‖ ≥ 1/‖η − θ‖, and a uniform lift bound C forces ‖η − θ‖ ≥ 1/C (general). The minimal-norm lift in G^⊥ of a coefficient vector v is ‖r‖⁻²r with r its residual against the other coefficients, of norm 1/‖r‖; on the actual weighted idele-class objects (all number fields, all unitary Hecke characters, |Re z| ≤ α, m < d, s = 2d) ‖r_i‖ ≤ ‖(η_i − η_j)/√w‖_{L²(μ)}. In log coordinates the dual coefficient difference satisfies ∫|e^{itu} − e^{ivu}|²e^{2bu−2α|u|}(1+u²)^{−s/2}du ≤ 2(t − v)²/δ³ in the strip interior |b| + δ ≤ α.
- *(a′) Lemma 10.4.2.5 in Hilbert form, for arbitrary families (no Schauder hypothesis).* If F ∩ G is dense in G, each coset containing an element of F contains elements of F within any δ > 0 of a prescribed representative p; if p ⊥ G and ‖u‖ ≤ ‖p‖ + ε for u in its coset, then ‖u − p‖ ≤ √(ε(2‖p‖ + ε)); lifts u_i ∈ F with ‖u_i − p_i‖‖η_i‖ ≤ ε·2^{−i} exist.
- *(b) Rank-one costs.* The orthogonal lift has exact cost ‖A(lift r)‖·‖v‖ = ‖A r‖‖v‖/‖r‖² (general; also for genuine jets on the actual objects). Rescaling (ψ̃, η) ↦ (cψ̃, c⁻¹η) leaves cost and operator unchanged. For any lift u and g ∈ G: 2‖A g‖‖η‖ ≤ ‖A(u+g)‖‖η‖ + ‖A(u−g)‖‖η‖ (general; pointwise for the genuine convolution on the idele class group). With lifts u_i in a collar ‖u_i‖ ≤ C, η_i(u_i) = 1, η_i ⊥ G, and g ∈ F ∩ G with A g ≠ 0, there are lifts v_i ∈ F in the same cosets with ‖v_i‖ ≤ C + ‖g‖, η_i(v_i) = 1 and Σ_i ‖A v_i‖‖η_i‖ = ∞; this is also proved on the genuine weighted idele-class space W_I^s = L²(C_K, w_{I,s}μ) with A the literal convolution (as hypothesis).
- *(b′) Translation estimate used for (10.4.2.34).* The printed bound (9.3.1.6) cannot bound a norm ≥ 1 at |g| = 1 (s > 0); the corrected weight bound w(u + v) ≤ e^{2α|u|}(2(1+u²))^{s/2}w(v), its integrated form, and the shear weight ratio of (10.4.2.28) hold, with no spectral-frequency decay in the constants.
- *(b″) Smoothed lifts.* For a convolved vector whose pairing with χ·(profile_t − profile_u) equals λ: |λ|² ≤ 4|t − u|²·V(∫(1+r²)⁻¹dr)·‖A f‖² (jet orders m + 1 < d, |b| ≤ α). Pointwise and energy bounds for genuine convolutions φ ∗ h hold with input energies and no label decay. Costs through the quotient Q and orthogonal section S are first-power costs that do not increase.
- *(c) Sum and footnote 8 (trace limit (10.4.2.20)).* Under the first-power hypothesis Σ_i ‖w·(φ ∗ q f_i)‖₂·‖c_i q⁻¹ ℓ^{n_i}/w‖₂ < ∞ and vanishing lower Mellin moments, the cutoff diagonals Σ_i ∫ρ_k·(φ ∗ q f_i)·c_i q⁻¹ ℓ^{n_i} dμ converge to Σ_i M(φ)(c_i q⁻¹)·M(f_i ℓ^{n_i})(c_i) (actual idele class group, lifts and characters as parameters). For any supplied nuclear expansion of P∘A (P = projection onto a closed E), uniformly bounded strongly convergent cutoffs give Σ_i⟨b_i, M_k P A b_i⟩ → Σ_n⟨u_n, v_n⟩ (general H). On the actual objects for both tests φ# and (ℓφ)#, a supplied nuclear expansion of A∘P^spec yields the expansion of P^spec∘A with no larger cost and Tr(P^spec A M_{ρn}) → Tr(P^spec A) for (Λ, ε)-masks.

<a id="issue-22"></a>
## Issue 22 — (10.4.2.21)–(10.4.2.23)

The jets used throughout the checked spectral identification are η = χ∣g∣^{b+it}(log∣g∣)^m with z = a+b+it (`genuineMellinJet K χ z a m`, docstring "Actual unnormalized Mellin jet from (10.2.2.4), with z-a=b+it"). Annihilation of the closed theta span by such a jet is proved when z is a zero of the supplied L of order > m (so the zero condition is at a+b+it), when b = Re z − a satisfies the strict admission ∣b∣ < α, or ∣b∣ = α with 2m+1 < s, and the admitted boundary orders are min(N, ⌈(s−1)/2⌉) (Issue 11). The binomial law η_m(g·x) = Σ_j C(m,j) η_j(g) η_{m−j}(x) of (10.4.2.29)–(10.4.2.30) is proved for this exponent on C_K; the measured convolution action on log-jets is proved for an arbitrary character c; a norm twist |·|^{it} shifts z by −it and preserves admission.

<a id="issue-23"></a>
## Issue 23 — Theorem 10.4.2.8, proof

- *Lifted sums in the order A∘P (general).* For any bounded A and P on a normed space and any lifts u_i with η_j(u_i) = δ_ij, the finite lifted sums T_J = Σ_{j∈J} η_j ⊗ A u_j satisfy (T_J − A∘P)(u_i) = A(u_i − P u_i) for i ∈ J, hence ‖A(u_i − P u_i)‖ ≤ ‖T_J − A∘P‖·‖u_i‖; if T_{range n} → A∘P in operator norm, then A(u_i − P u_i) = 0 for every i. With P = P^spec this is the condition A(φ)P^geom ψ̃_i = 0.
- *Order identities (general).* P∘A∘(I − P) = 0 (equivalently P A = P A P) implies A∘P = P∘A + (I − P)∘A∘P; P A = P A P holds when A preserves G (P = P_{G^⊥}).
- *On the actual objects.* For every number field K, interval [l, r], s ≥ 0 and φ continuous with compact support, both tests φ# and (ℓφ)# satisfy P^spec∘A∘P_W = 0 with W the full theta closure and P^spec = 1 − P_W (convolution preserves W). For finite theta-source spans E_T ⊆ W: Tr(P_{E_T}∘A∘P^spec) = 0, both tests.
- *Transfer of a supplied A∘P^spec expansion to P^spec∘A.* If P A P_S = 0, then P(A P) = P A; any nuclear expansion A∘P = Σ⟨u_j,·⟩v_j gives P∘A = Σ⟨u_j,·⟩P v_j with Σ‖u_j‖‖P v_j‖ ≤ Σ‖u_j‖‖v_j‖ (general). On the actual objects, for both tests, a supplied A∘P^spec expansion yields the P^spec∘A expansion, its cost bound, an integral kernel whose diagonal integral is Tr(P^spec∘A), and the masked limit Tr(P^spec A M_{ρn}) → Tr(P^spec A).
- *Off-diagonal trace (general).* For F idempotent, E F = 0 and F A E trace class: Tr(F A E) = 0.
- *Orthogonal lifts, P∘A order.* A quotient expansion of Ā transfers through Q and the orthogonal section S (S∘Q = P_{G^⊥}) to P^spec∘A with outputs S v_i, without cost increase; S∘(f ⊗ v)∘Q = (f∘Q) ⊗ S v. Finite windows: projecting biorthogonal representatives onto the window gives exactly P_J∘A; limits of blocks with eventual jet agreement have the same projection P_{G^⊥}∘(·) as A; exhausting finite matrices converge strongly to P^spec∘A.
- *Lift corrections.* If F ∩ G is dense in G, lifts u_i ∈ F in the cosets of prescribed representatives p_i can be chosen so that the correction Σ_i η_i ⊗ A(u_i − p_i) converges to an operator R with ‖R‖ ≤ 2ε‖A‖ and nuclear cost ≤ 2ε‖A‖ (general; also on the weighted idele-class space with A the literal convolution, with left-cutoff versions). Conversely, if A g ≠ 0 for some g ∈ F ∩ G, lifts in the same cosets with non-summable cost exist. Near-minimal lifts are within √(ε(2‖p‖ + ε)) of the orthogonal representative p.

<a id="issue-24"></a>
## Issue 24 — (10.4.3.1)

For K = ℚ and χ = ∣·∣^{iτ} with τ ≠ 0, the completed function Λ(s) = completedRiemannZeta(s+iτ) is differentiable and nonzero at s = 0 while the unshifted product s(s−1)Λ(s) vanishes there (a zero not present in the cleared function), and s(s−1)Λ(s) is not continuous at the shifted pole s = −iτ. The shifted clearing polynomial (z+iτ)(z−(1−iτ)) is entire and nonzero on Re z > 1; dividing an entire F with F(−iτ) ≠ 0 and F(1−iτ) ≠ 0 by it produces simple poles exactly at −iτ and 1−iτ and leaves every other order unchanged; for a meromorphic Λ attached by a half-plane identity Z = (z+iτ)(z−(1−iτ))Λ, ord_x Λ = ord_x Z − [x = −iτ] − [x = 1−iτ]; for a Mathlib WeakFEPair with k = 1 and nonzero constant terms, the shifted Λ(z+iτ) has simple poles at both points and the cleared function has no added zeros there. For ℚ, the cleared function (s+iτ)(s+iτ−1)Λ(s) (with its removable values) is entire with value 1 at both shifted poles.

| Declaration | Proves | Objects | Scope | Hypotheses |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MasulloGRH.unshiftedClearing_spurious_zero` | Λ_τ differentiable at 0, Λ_τ(0) ≠ 0, s(s−1)Λ_τ(s) = 0 at s = 0, rationalHeckeXi τ 0 ≠ 0 | `rationalHeckeCompleted τ s = completedRiemannZeta (s + iτ)` (Mathlib) | K = ℚ, pure norm twists | τ ≠ 0 |
| `MasulloGRH.unshiftedClearing_not_continuous` | s(s−1)Λ_τ(s) is not continuous at −iτ | same | K = ℚ | τ ≠ 0 |

<a id="issue-25"></a>
## Issue 25 — (10.4.3.43)–(10.4.3.45)

1. On the actual objects, for every number field K and both tests, with no hypothesis beyond l ≤ r, s ≥ 0 and φ continuous, compactly supported and vanishing on the norm-one classes, `PIntegrated.geometric_trace_global_operator_assembly` proves:
- the spectral compression of A on W⊥, extended by zero on W, equals P∘A (the factorization (10.4.2.5), P^spec∘A = S_a∘A^spec∘Q_a, in the Hilbert realization);
- for an orthonormal Hilbert basis (e_i) of W⊥ = G⊥ and every input F, Σ_i ⟨A* e_i, F⟩ e_i converges (HasSum) to P A F. This is the coefficient tensor Σ_i A′e_i ⊗ e_i of (10.4.3.42)–(10.4.3.43), with convolution in the functional slot, and its action is P∘A;
- P∘A∘S = 0 (G is A-invariant, derived from the translation action on the admitted Schwartz–Bruhat tests) and A∘P − P∘A = S∘A∘P, i.e. the two orders differ by the block (1−P)AP = P^geom A P^spec of Issue 25;
- every character's theta closure lies in W;
- at finite level, the residual kernel k_T(x,y) = h(xy⁻¹) − K_T(x,y) (free convolution kernel minus the kernel K_T of P_{E_T}∘A, the Lean analogue of the subtraction in (10.4.3.50)) represents (1 − P_{E_T})∘A on every input, is L²-admissible, and these operators converge strongly to P∘A as T ↑; with masks, M_ρ(1 − P_{E_T})A F and M_ρ(1 − P_{E_T})A M_ρ F converge to P A F jointly along cutoffRegime × (T ↑) for every mask family satisfying the window condition.
2. The same order is proved at finite rank on the actual objects with genuine Mellin jets η_i = χ_i(x)|x|^{z_i − a}(log|x|)^{m_i}: the kernel Σ_i u_i(x)·(A′η_i)(y), (A′η_i)(y) = ∫ h(g) η_i(g y) dμ(g), acts on every weighted input f as Σ_i ⟨η_i, A f⟩ u_i (`Masullo.TransposeDiagonalIntegrable.actual_finite_PA_kernel_action`: "It is P_F A, not A P_F"); with inverse-Gram dual columns this kernel is the orthogonal projection onto span{η_i-Riesz vectors} applied after A (`Masullo.GramCompletedKernelAction.actual_Gram_completed_kernel_action`, `Masullo.InverseGramProjection.actual_completed_orthogonal_PA_action`).
3. General-space versions: at every finite stage the tensor Σ (ℓ_i∘A) ⊗ u_i acts as v ↦ P_N(A v) and the tensor Σ ℓ_i ⊗ A u_i acts as A∘P_N (`TraceRepairE.coefficient_kernel_is_PA`, `TraceRepairE.output_kernel_is_AP`); an operator whose coordinates are η_i(A x) and whose range is fixed by p equals p∘a (`NativePWCorrection.tensor_order_by_coordinates`); biorthogonal finite windows give P_J∘A exactly and converge strongly to P^spec∘A (`Round177F.*`, `Round176F.*`); a nuclear factorization of the compression on S⊥ is a nuclear factorization of P∘A, with trace(P∘A) = trace(compression), and its kernel represents P∘A with diagonal integral trace(P∘A) (`HOperatorTrace.source_compression_trace`, `DNuclearKernel.source_compression_kernel`).
4. Where the A∘P order is used, it enters as an explicit hypothesis: given a nuclear expansion of A∘P on the actual objects, the checked declarations derive a nuclear expansion of P∘A with projected outputs, a kernel of P∘A on every input with diagonal integral trace(P∘A), and trace(P∘A∘M_{ρ_n}) → trace(P∘A) (`Q.FullSourceCutoff.both_tests_PA_physical_limit_of_AP`, `DAllSourceKernel.both_tests_originalHaar_projected_kernel_of_AP`). The declarations `MasulloTrace.geometric_action_after_projection` / `truncated_geometric_left_action` take "S represents A∘P" as a hypothesis and conclude that φ(xy⁻¹) − S represents A∘(1−P) (with the mask ρ(x) on the left).
5. The block (1−P)AP and cutoffs: on the actual objects, for both tests, given a nuclear expansion of A∘P, the block R = S∘A∘P has trace 0 and trace(R∘M_p) → 0, trace(M_p∘R) → 0 along the cutoff regime with the smooth masks (`Q.FullSourceCutoff.both_tests_full_order_remainder_physical_limit`); for each fixed finite source span E_T ⊂ G, with no nuclear input, the finite kernel of P_{E_T}∘(A∘P) (printed order A∘P^spec, compressed to E_T) has integrable diagonal with integral 0, trace(P_{E_T}∘A∘P) = 0, and with the smooth manuscript masks (C^∞ in log|x|, equal to 1 on the window, multiplicatively symmetric) the masked diagonal integral and trace(P_{E_T}∘A∘P∘M_z) tend to 0 along the cutoff regime (`AReview.ArithmeticOrder.both_tests_full_source_order_trace_zero`, `…_smooth_limit`). For B trace class (genuine trace-norm theory) and Q idempotent, trace(QB(1−Q)M) = trace(B(1−Q)(MQ − QM)) and trace(QBM) − trace(QBQM) → 0 under strongly convergent contractive-defect cutoffs (`CorrectionCutoffDefectR01.*`); 2×2 matrix examples show a nilpotent block with trace 0 acquiring trace 1/4 after a cutoff, and cyclic trace equality not determining diagonal entries.

## Issue 26 — (10.4.3.47), (10.4.3.30)

- Multiplying a kernel by ρ(x) gives the kernel of M_ρ composed with the kernel's operator, at finite level on the actual objects: ρ(x)·b(x)∫ k_T(x,y)F(y)/b(y) dμ(y) = M_ρ(1 − P_{E_T})A F a.e., with L² admission, for k_T = h(xy⁻¹) − K_T, and these operators converge to P^spec A F jointly in (cutoff regime) × (T ↑) (`KPhysicalMask.both_tests_full_masked_kernel_net`); the two-sided kernel ρ(x)k_T(x,y)ρ(y) represents M_ρ(1 − P_{E_T})A M_ρ with the same joint limit (`DAllSourceKernel.both_tests_full_twosided_kernel_net`).
- The kernel built from a nuclear factorization (of the compression on G⊥, or of A∘P with projected outputs) represents P^spec∘A on every input (`DNuclearKernel.source_compression_kernel`, `DAllSourceKernel.both_tests_originalHaar_projected_kernel_of_AP`, Issue 25).
- Conditional statement on the idele class group with a weight w: if S(x,·)/w ∈ L², the input is in the weighted L², and S represents A∘P at x, then ρ(x)·(φ(xy⁻¹) − S(x,y)) integrates against f to ρ(x)·(A(f − Pf))(x) (`MasulloTrace.weighted_truncated_geometric_action`, docstring "The weighted Hilbert-space version needed for (10.4.3.47)-(10.4.3.48)"); for compact continuous φ and continuous w the convolution-row hypothesis is discharged (`MasulloTrace.weighted_truncated_action_of_compact_test`); w⁻¹ in the kernel cancels against the measure wμ (`MasulloTrace.weighted_geometric_kernel_action`).
- One-variable a.e. uniqueness: two integrable functions with equal integrals over every finite-measure measurable set (or with the same localized traces) are a.e. equal (`ActualRowDensityR06.density_unique`, `TraceDensityUniquenessR05.density_unique_of_localized_trace`).

## Issue 27 — Proposition 11.1.1.2, Corollary 11.1.1.4

- Orbit sums on the actual ideles. For b supported in a compact S ⊂ A_K and y in a compact Y ⊂ A_K^×, one finite U ⊂ K^× (depending on S and Y, not on b) contains every u with u·y ∈ S; Σ_{u∈K^×} b(u·y) converges, equals the sum over U, and is bounded by #U·sup‖b‖; the orbit sums are continuous in y; weighted L² bounds hold on bounded log-slabs.
- Schwartz–Bruhat tests. For every finite tensor sum of archimedean Schwartz and finite-adelic locally constant compactly supported functions, the orbit series is summable at every idele. For a finite factor ψ supported in a compact C with ‖ψ‖ ≤ M, the absolute orbit sum Σ_{ξ∈F^×}‖ψ(a_f ξ)f(t a_∞ ξ)‖ is ≤ M·B·‖f‖_{k,0}·t^{−N} for t ≥ 1, a in a compact U, with k, B chosen from (C, U, N) before f, ψ, M. Termwise majorants of the archimedean lattice sums, uniform in a Schwartz seminorm ball, are proved.
- Dominated convergence in termwise-majorant form. In the §12.3 setting: on A_K^× × K^× (counting measure on K^×), indicator carvings of an integrable lifted diagonal converge to its S-idele part (majorant ‖F‖); the K^×-orbit sums of the lifted diagonal with nonnegative normalized weights are absolutely convergent with sum d([x]). Generic lemmas: sums with a summable majorant uniform in the cutoff parameter converge to the uncut sum.

<a id="issue-28"></a>
## Issue 28 — (11.2.1.2), (11.2.1.4)

- For arbitrary bounded L, A and any bounded R_n converging strongly to the geometric projection 1 − P_E (P_E the spectral projection), the statement "L(1−P_E)A R_n x → L(1−P_E)A x for every x" (the printed (11.2.1.4)/(11.2.1.9) in strong form, with L = M_ρ) implies L(1−P_E)A P_E = 0, i.e. M_ρ P_geom A P_spec = 0 (`FollowupD30.geometric_truncation_full_limit_forces_offDiagonal`).
- With right truncations Q_T = projections onto finite spans of source vectors exhausting the closed source span (range of S = P_geom): M S A Q_T x → M S A x − M S A(x − S x) = M S A S x for every bounded M, A (`Q.IntegratedSourceCutoff.right_source_condensation_with_remainder`). For an operator T with a nuclear expansion: trace(T Q_T) → trace(T S) and trace(T Q_T) + trace(T(1 − S)) → trace(T) (`Q.IntegratedSourceCutoff.right_source_operator_trace_with_remainder`, whose docstring of the inner lemma reads "For the manuscript application A is the masked geometric operator M S A_h").
- Ring identities (any ring): S A S = A S and S W = W give M S A W = M A W; S A S = A S gives M S A − M A S = M S A (1 − S); M S A − M S A S = M S A (1 − S) (`NativePWCondensation.*`, `NativePWCorrection.condensation_defect`). With S = P_geom, A = A(φ), M = M_ρ, W = Q_L these are M_ρ P_g A Q_L = M_ρ A Q_L and M_ρ P_g A − M_ρ A P_g = M_ρ P_g A P_spec.
- The hypothesis S A S = A S is proved on the actual objects for both tests (`OAllCharacterClosure.both_tests_full_PA_source_zero`), and finite subsets of the actual theta family give P_{F_T} y → S y for every y (`OAllCharacterClosure.actual_full_theta_source_exhaustion`; generic: `Masullo.NestedProjection.global_projection_tendsto`, nested finite subspaces dense in E give strong convergence to the projection onto E, not to Id).
- The defect trace: on the actual objects for both tests, given a nuclear expansion of A∘P^spec, trace(M_p∘P_geom∘A∘P_spec) → 0 and trace(P_geom∘A∘P_spec∘M_p) → 0 along the cutoff regime with the smooth masks, and trace(P_geom∘A∘P_spec) = 0 (`Q.FullSourceCutoff.both_tests_full_order_remainder_physical_limit`, Issue 25). With T = M_p∘P_geom∘A, trace(M_p∘P_geom∘A∘P_spec) = Tr(T∘P_spec) is the defect of the Issue 28 derivation (there rewritten as Tr(P_spec M_ρ P_geom A) by cyclicity).
- On the actual objects the left-side counterpart is proved: M_ρ(1 − P_{E_T})A F → P^spec A F jointly in the cutoff regime and T ↑ (conjunct (c) of `PIntegrated.geometric_trace_global_operator_assembly`, Issue 25).

| Declaration | Proves | Objects | Scope | Hypotheses |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `FollowupD30.geometric_truncation_full_limit_forces_offDiagonal` | ∀x, L(A(P_E x) − P_E(A(P_E x))) = 0 | General complex Hilbert space H, closed E (spectral subspace; x − P_E x is the geometric part), bounded A, L, R_n (parameters) | full operators; strong convergence | hR: ∀x, R_n x → x − P_E x; hfull: ∀x, L(A(R_n x) − P_E A(R_n x)) → L(A x − P_E A x). <a id="issue-29"></a>
## Issue 29 — §11.3.1, primary divisorial stratification

Every checked definition of a divisorial stratum or mask on ideles is a componentwise valuation-unit condition at individual finite places: along an arbitrary sequence of places v: ℕ → FinitePlace K, one place at a time (`NDivisorialShells.stratum`, `GeometricC.unitCarving`, which also excludes a set S), or for arbitrary finite sets P of places (`GeometricReview18C.UnitStratum`, `Geometric19C.UnitStratum`, `MasulloGRH.GeometricReview.finiteUnitMask`), with the S-ideles {units at every v ∉ S} as residual. Proved facts about these sets: closed and clopen in the idele topology, measurable, antitone in n, the indicator differences are minus the disjoint shells, the masks are continuous, and the residual shell is the S-idele set when the range of v is the complement of S. The `GeometricC.unitCarving` docstring states its relation to the norm map: "These are not inverse images under a field norm." No declaration defines a stratum through a norm map to ℚ, saturated sets of places, or places above rational primes.

| Declaration | Proves | Objects | Scope | Hypotheses |

Docstrings: "The genuine componentwise unit restrictions in (11.3.1.2). ## Issue 30 — (11.3.1.4), used in the proof of Theorem 12.5.1.1

- Paper convention (α_{k+1} − α_k)k. α_n k = α_0 k + Σ_{j<n}(α_{j+1} − α_j)k; α_{n+1} − α_n = −1_{shell n} pointwise on ideles. Integrated: for every integrable d on C_K and the descended masks, Σ_n ∫(α̃_{n+1} − α̃_n)d dµ converges absolutely to ∫ p_none·d − ∫ d, i.e. total = residual − Σ(paper-sign shells). The same identity holds on the lifted diagonal over A_K^× × K^× (C13), and for the actual finite-level residual kernel of E_T for both tests, on the ideles with the Haar scalar c (positive shells sum to −c·tr(P_{E_T}A), paper-sign shells to ∫(residual piece) + c·tr(P_{E_T}A)) and on C_K.
- Positive convention (α_k − α_{k+1})k. Σ_{n<N}(α_{P_n} − α_{P_{n+1}})k = (1 − α_{P_N})k and k = β_S·k + Σ_n(positive shells) eventually (Geometric19C); every partition theorem uses nonnegative pieces with Σ_e p_e = 1, i.e. ∫d = residual + Σ positive shells, including the global assembly.
- Algebraic sign lemmas: (next − current)k = −((current − next)k), with docstring "Manuscript (11.3.1.4): positive strata have the opposite shell sign."
