<a id="issue-31"></a>
## Issue 31 — Theorem 11.3.2.1 vs Theorem 12.5.1.1; footnote in §12.4.1

- Test hypothesis. Every checked orbital-trace and trace statement on the actual objects assumes φ continuous with compact support; the residual-kernel, cutoff and local-term statements also assume φ(C_K^1) = 0 (`classLog K x = 0 → phi x = 0`); the P∘A orbital trace has no norm-one condition (it takes a nuclear expansion as input); the finite-rank orbital traces have no test. Support on both sides of C_K^1 is allowed throughout. No checked statement assumes support in C_K^{>1}. On the actual finite-level residual kernel of E_T, for both tests and all K, the tensor-indented orbital diagonal is absolutely integrable on the ideles with integral −c·tr(P_{E_T}A); for the full operator P∘A the orbital diagonal integrates to c·tr(P∘A), given a nuclear expansion of A∘P as input. The value is a trace, not the local value (log q_v/µ_v(O_v^×))Σ_k φ(π_v^k) of (11.3.2.1).
- Support transport. φ supported over C^{>1} gives φ#, (ℓφ)# supported over C^{<1}, and conversely.
- Large places, two-sided support. A test vanishing on norm one with support in the band T⁻¹ < ‖x‖ < T vanishes at every norm q^k (q > T) (GeometricC); on actual C_K, for both tests, all lattice samples h(gⁿ) vanish when abs(ℓ(g)) exceeds the support radius, and the finite local terms vanish outside a finite set of places.
- Footnote 2. The max-log identity log λ − log max{w/λ′, 1/λ″} = log(λλ′) − log w − max{0, log(λ′/(wλ″))} is proved for independent λ, λ′, λ″ > 0. Dominated-convergence limits of the lower-cutoff-renormalized logarithm against a supplied integrand with a logarithmic (or two half-power) moment are proved along λ′/λ″ → 0, R = λλ′ → ∞ (scalar), and along independent endpoints a = log(λλ′) → ∞, b = log(λ′/λ″) → −∞ on the actual adele ring with the actual S-local modulus, where the divergent term cancels by ∫h = φ(1) = 0. For λ″ = λ′ a nonzero correction −log r on 0 < r < 1 is proved to remain. Smooth asymmetric cutoffs with support [1/D, U], U and D independent, are constructed on C_K and their removal is proved for any integrable density and for the actual finite-level kernels.

## Issue 32 — (11.3.2.3)

- Annihilation. For finite sets P ⊆ Q ⊆ R of finite places, every kernel k on pairs of actual ideles and all x, y: ((α_Q(y) − α_P(y))·k(x, y))·α_R(y) = 0, where α_P(y) = 1 iff y is a unit at every v ∈ P. The masked shell equals the shell iff the shell value is 0. Two independent checks.
- Limit of the masks and corrected decomposition. Along a monotone sequence P_n exhausting the finite places outside S, unitMask(P_n) = outsideMask_S eventually (outsideMask_S = indicator of units at every v ∉ S, the β_S of the derivation); with P_0 = ∅, outsideMask_S·k + Σ_{n<N}(α_{P_n} − α_{P_{n+1}})·k = k eventually in N; integrated along the diagonal and along indented pairs (x, principal(a)x), the positive shell sums tend to ∫(1 − outsideMask_S)·k.
- Descent. If some principal idele is a non-unit at a place outside S, the raw mask outsideMask_S is not the pullback of any function on C_K; multiplication by an idele that is a unit outside S preserves the outside-S unit set.
- Further: `α_{n+1} − α_n = −1_{shell n}` pointwise; the incoming-point corrections (mask at x minus mask at xt⁻¹) sum to 0 over all shells, absolutely, with a mass bound. No declaration multiplies a later stratum mask by a shell.

## Issue 35 — Theorem 12.5.1.1, (12.5.1.4)

- Exact divisor accounting with true orders and the strict boundary rule 2j+1 < s, for the zeros of an arbitrary function f and arbitrary weights W: at every finite height and (under absolute summability of N·W) for the infinite sum, full = native + omitted. Exterior zeros enter the omitted part with full order N; a boundary zero enters with N − #{j < N: 2j+1 < s}; at width 0 a zero on the centre line keeps #{j < N: 2j+1 < s}; at s = 1 no boundary jet is kept.
- Given the full explicit formula G = P − Σ N·W, the corrected formula G = P − Σ n·W − Σ (N − n)·W holds, and the native formula G = P − Σ n·W holds if and only if Σ (N − n)·W = 0 (in Issue 35 notation: iff R − B_adm = 0). In scalar form: from T = J + B and G + O = P − T, the strict-interior formula G = P − J holds iff O + B = 0 (with O = R − B_adm, B = B_adm: iff R = 0).
- Two widths a < b: native(b) − native(a) = omitted(a) − omitted(b), at finite height, for the infinite sums, and for limits; for boundary-free widths the same identity holds for the raw strict-interior sums; with free boundaries the annulus sum a ≤ |Re − c| < b equals the limit of the omitted-height difference.
- Joined to an operator on a general Hilbert space: with P the orthogonal projection onto a closed subspace E, a nuclear expansion of P∘A and a triangular action of A on a Hilbert basis of E with one vector per admitted jet and diagonal W, geometric = pole − Σ_n ⟨u_n, v_n⟩ − Σ (N − n)·W.
- Width independence of the remainder is equivalent to the critical-line conclusion: for entire f ≢ 0 with hcount, if the interior-strip height limits of N·concreteTransform equal G − remainder(a) on boundary-free widths a, then remainder independent of the width ⟺ every zero has real part 1/2. With the remainder taken to be the explicit omitted squared resolvent Σ (N − n)(z − (ρ − c))^{−2}: width independence ⟺ every zero has real part c (no strip-confinement hypothesis); the same for differences of native logged sums under a one-sided real-part bound.
- For the concrete logged test (entire f, hcount, Re(ρ − c) ≤ b, Re z > b): the full sum Σ N·concreteTransform converges to Σ N (z − (ρ − c))^{−2}, and the native logged sum converges to G iff G = full limit − omitted squared resolvent.
- For K = Q (zeros of Mathlib's completedRiemannZeta), both test families (plain, logged), PA operator: from the interfaces SpectralIdentification and GeometricTrace (geometric trace = arithmetic term − (Σ omitted − Σ edge)), the full explicit formula Σ N·M(φ)(ρ − 1/2) = arithmetic term follows; at two widths the strip corrections differ by the difference of native traces, and equal corrections ⟺ equal native traces.
- On the constructed idele class group, every unitary Hecke character, both required tests φ# and (ℓφ)#: at a boundary point Re z ∈ {a + l, a + r} the number of orders m < N for which the genuine normalized Mellin jet lies in L²(μ) equals min(N, ⌈(s−1)/2⌉) (= #{m < N: 2m+1 < s}), and retained + omitted multiplicities times the test's Mellin coefficient give N times it.

## Issue 36 — Chapter 13; §14.1.2

No checked declaration involves the cap min(N(c), [F:Q]), the vanishing conditions (13.3.1.2), or a statement of Theorem 13.3.1.2. The checked declarations named after Chapter 13 are and concern K = ℚ through an abstract record `RationalTraceData` (test space, Hilbert spaces, operators, theta and correction functions are fields of the record): zeros are the zeros of completedRiemannZeta other than 0, 1 with their full analytic order; jets are admitted by the strict boundary rule. Under the record's spectral-identification and geometric-trace predicates, the full explicit formula with full multiplicities holds; under the Chapter 13 regularized-limit predicate, the paired correction-control predicate is equivalent to RH, and at every zero q with Re q > 1/2 there is a width whose logged correction is unbounded near q. Conditional critical-line deductions for supplied functions are checked separately (listed below).

## Issue 39 — Proposition B.4.3.2, Definition B.4.4.1

- At finite places, on tests φ on the constructed idele class group that vanish on the local norm-one set: Weil's principal-value expression PF0 of (B.4.4.5), log(N v)·(∫ off the norm-one shell of φ(x)|x|^{1/2}/|1 − x| + ∫ over the shell of (φ(y) − φ(1))|y|^{1/2}/|1 − y|), equals the unregularized geometric local term log(N v)·∫ φ(x)|x|^{1/2}/|1 − x| dμ_v. For tests supported in 1/T < |x|_v < T at every v, vanishing on norm one and continuous, the family of local PF0 values is summable and its sum is the finite sum of geometric terms over places with N v ≤ T. The local integrand vanishes on a neighbourhood of 1 and equals φ·|x|^{1/2}/max(1, |x|).
- Both required tests φ# and (ℓφ)# vanish on classLog = 0 when φ does.
- At the real place, an explicit counterterm repair for the concrete kernel (Issue 1 row realPlaceRegularized_*), and the raw truncated integrals are unbounded (Issue 1).

## Issue 40 — Lemmas B.4.4.8–B.4.4.9, (B.4.4.16)

- Counting hypothesis ⇒ summability: a weighted polynomial count Σ_{h_i ≤ T} µ_i ≤ C T^c gives Σ µ_i h_i^{−s} < ∞ for s > c; a weighted T(1 + log T) count gives Σ µ_i/h_i² < ∞; for a locally finite family w_i ∈ ℂ with weights m_i, a T(1 + log T) count by modulus gives Σ‖m_i‖/‖w_i‖² < ∞; a dyadic count with multiplicity retained gives a summable inverse-square divisor.
- Zeros with multiplicity: for a nonzero entire f, a multiplicity-weighted R(1 + log R) zero count gives Σ_z ord_z(f)/‖z − c‖² < ∞, and (zeros in a half-plane) Σ_z ord_z(f)·‖∫ e^{−(z0−(z−c))x}·x·χ_{n+2}(x)dx‖ < ∞ for the scalar cutoff test of the conditional argument. Height truncations of multiplicity-weighted sums are finite without a count; zero sets of nonzero entire functions are finite in discs.
- Dirichlet L-functions (K = ℚ): multiplicity-weighted zero counts up to height T are bounded by circle averages of log‖L‖ (Jensen); the polynomial-growth input is a hypothesis in the band version.
- Limit passage and bookkeeping: uniform convergence of multiplicity-weighted regularized resolvent sums given Σ m_i/(1 + ‖u_i‖²) < ∞; dominated limit passage in weighted zero sums under a uniform inverse-square bound; splitting the full-multiplicity sum into selected and omitted parts.

<a id="issue-41"></a>
## Issue 41 — Lemma C.3.4.4(2)

The two one-sided ideal inequalities are proved separately, in three formalisms; none states the equality ‖AT‖ = ‖TA‖.
- Trace norm: traceNorm(C∘T) ≤ ‖C‖·traceNorm(T) and traceNorm(T∘C) ≤ ‖C‖·traceNorm(T) for all bounded C, T; trace class is closed under left and right composition; traceNorm(T†) = traceNorm(T); trace(C∘T) = trace(T∘C) for trace-class T (equality of traces). Hilbert–Schmidt analogues hold on rectangular compositions.
- Rank-one representation cost: left composition with a contraction M keeps a summable rank-one representation and does not increase its cost; right composition with Q multiplies the cost bound by ‖Q‖.
- Quotient form used for (10.4.2.5) and at p. 459: the cost of S∘(f⊗v)∘Q is ≤ ‖f‖‖v‖, series costs are equal through S(−)Q, and nuclearRepresentationCost(P_{G^⊥}∘A) ≤ nuclearRepresentationCost(Ā).
The adjoint of a rank-one ⟨v,·⟩u is ⟨u,·⟩v. All statements concern operators given as parameters on an abstract Hilbert space.

## Issue 42 — Lemma C.3.6.3(1)

No checked declaration defines singular values or approximation numbers σ_k, or Schatten norms S_p for p other than 1 (trace norm) and 2 (Hilbert–Schmidt), and none states a singular-value inequality for a sum (same-index, shifted-index, Lipschitz or product form). The sum estimates that are checked are norm-level: the trace-norm triangle inequality traceNorm(S+T) ≤ traceNorm(S) + traceNorm(T) (the p = 1 case of the S_p triangle inequality invoked at p. 706); for Hilbert–Schmidt norms ‖S+T‖²_HS ≤ 2‖S‖²_HS + 2‖T‖²_HS and ‖T‖ ≤ ‖T‖_HS; and, for finite rank-one sums, ‖Σ_{i∈s}⟨η_i,·⟩v_i‖ ≤ Σ_{i∈s}‖η_i‖‖v_i‖ together with an explicit finite rank-one representation of cost Σ‖v_i‖‖u_i‖ that also bounds the operator norm. These are the finite rank-one costings used in place of (C.3.6.13) by the reviewed finite-block arguments. All are stated for operators given as parameters on an abstract Hilbert space.

<a id="issue-43"></a>
## Issue 43 — Proposition C.3.6.5, proof

Cases of the proposition are proved for p = 1 (trace norm or rank-one cost) and p = 2 (Hilbert–Schmidt), with operators as parameters on an abstract complex Hilbert space:
- Right composition, finite rank: for A = Σ_{i<d}⟨v_i,·⟩u_i and self-adjoint P_j → Id strongly along any filter, A∘P_j − A = Σ⟨P_j v_i − v_i,·⟩u_i has finite rank-one cost Σ‖P_j v_i − v_i‖‖u_i‖ → 0, and ‖A∘P_j − A‖ → 0 (Round173E); the same with orthogonal projections P_{U_n}η_i → η_i (Round173C). Strong convergence of the finite jet-window projections is proved separately (Round175F).
- Left composition, trace norm: if A∘R = B∘C with B, C Hilbert–Schmidt and Q_n, Q are bounded with ‖(Q_n − Q)x‖ ≤ ‖x‖ and (Q_n − Q)x → 0, then traceNorm((Q_n − Q)∘A∘R) → 0; for contractive Q_n → 0 strongly, traceNorm(Q_n∘A∘B) → 0 and ‖Q_n∘A‖_HS → 0. Every trace-class operator is a product of two Hilbert–Schmidt operators.
- Left composition with nested finite-dimensional projections, rank-one-cost norm: for A∘M with a summable rank-one representation, nuclearNorm(((1−P_S)∘A∘M) − ((1−P_{E_T})∘A∘M)) → 0 as the finite spans E_T exhaust S, with explicit cost Σ_j‖u_j‖‖(P_{E_T} − P_S)v_j‖; this is applied to the interval convolution with both required tests and the full theta closure.
- The right-composition bounds needed in (C.3.6.14): traceNorm(T∘C) ≤ ‖C‖·traceNorm(T); ‖T∘B‖²_HS ≤ ‖B‖²‖T‖²_HS (rectangular); rank-one cost of T∘Q ≤ ‖Q‖·C; and for errors Err_n of rank-one cost → 0 and ‖Q_n‖ ≤ M, the cost of Err_n∘Q_n → 0.
- Trace-level consequences (trace(T∘P_a) → trace T; one- and two-sided cutoff trace limits).

<a id="issue-44"></a>
## Issue 44 — Converse argument (Chapters 3, 7, 10)

For any topological vector space V with continuous functionals d, q and pivots u, v in a dense submodule D with d u = 1, q u = 0, d v = 0, q v = 1, the correction x ↦ x − d(x)u − q(x)v maps D into D ∩ ker d ∩ ker q, fixes ker d ∩ ker q, and D ∩ ker d ∩ ker q is dense in ker d ∩ ker q; a continuous functional on ker d ∩ ker q vanishing on its D-points vanishes identically. Pivots are produced from two elements of D with value 1 at 0 and distinct Fourier-at-0 values; for the actual real Gaussians e^{−πx²} and e^{−4πx²} this calibration holds (values 1, 1; integral difference −1/2). On the archimedean side, S₀ ∩ 𝓢(mixedSpace K) is a closed subspace cut out by the continuous map f ↦ (f(0), f̂(0)). Lattice theta sums over a fractional ideal are continuous in the full mixed Schwartz test, with an explicit seminorm bound and a uniform dilation decay bound. On C_K, two-sided rapid decay of the class theta function follows from f(0) = f̂(0) = 0, the Poisson identity and the one-sided bounds (given as hypotheses); for original Bruhat tests the positive half of normalizedTheta lies in the weighted L² space. Continuity of theta S₀ → W_I^s enters the converse theorems as a hypothesis.
